ੴ ਸਤਿਗੁਰ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ॥

ਗਉੜੀ ਸੁਖਮਨੀ ਮਹਲਾ 5                 

Ek Onkar Satgur Persad(i) 

Gaurri Sukhmani Mehla 5

                                       

NOTE: Sukhmani Sahib is a very popular holy composition in Guru Granth Sahib, authored by Guru Arjun Dev Ji, the Fifth Master. It is mostly in praise of Waheguru and Satguru in very simple language. Some groups organize group recitations of the holy Bani. 

There is also a story about the holy composition: Guru Arjun Sahib had planned 24 Ashtpudies for the Bani, but after the 16th Ashtpudi, He could not continue and approached Baba Sri Chand, the elder son of Guru Nanak Dev Ji, for guidance. Baba Sri Chand advised to start the 17th Ashtpudi with the Sloak:  Aad(i) Sach(u) Joogaad(i) Sach(u). Haiy Bh(i)  Sach(u), Nanak Hosi Bh(i)  Sach(u). 

When Guru Arjun Dev Ji did that, He was able to complete the next 8 Ashtpudies. 

The story does not seem true for several reasons. The most compelling reason is that Guru Arjun Dev Ji was a spiritual incarnation of Guru Nanak Dev Ji; so, why would “Guru Nanak Dev Ji” go to Baba Sri Chand, His son, for guidance -especially when Sri Chand Ji had a different philosophy than Guru Nanak Sahib? So, the story is certainly not true. Guru Arjun Sahib did not need to seek any external advice to create the holy Gurbani. 

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਆਦਿ ਗੁਰਹ ਨਮਹ॥ ਜੁਗਾਦਿ ਗੁਰਹ ਨਮਹ॥ ਸਤਿਗੁਰਏ ਨਮਹ॥ ਸ੍ਰੀ ਗੁਰਦੇਵਏ ਨਮਹ॥ 1॥

Sloak(u): Aad(i) Gureh Numeh.  Jugad(i) Gureh Numeh. Satgureh Numeh.  Sri Gurdeveh Numeh. 

Word Meaning: Aad(i) = the beginning; the origin.  Numeh(i) = bowing respectfully. 

MEANING: I bow respectfully to the Guru which is the origin of everything. I bow to the Guru which was there before the eons.  I bow to the Satguru.  I bow to the Great Guru.   1.

ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਸਿਮਰਉ ਸਿਮਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਿ ਸੁਖੁ ਪਾਵਉ ॥ ਕਲਿ ਕਲੇਸ ਤਨ ਮਾਹਿ ਮਿਟਾਵਉ॥ ਸਿਮਰਉ ਜਾਸੁ ਬਿਸੰਭਰ ਏਕੈ॥ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਅਗਨਤ ਅਨੇਕੈ॥ ਬੇਦ ਪੁਰਾਨ ਸਿੰਮ੍ਰਿਤਿ ਸੁਧਾਖ੍ਹਰ॥ ਕੀਨੇ ਰਾਮ ਨਾਮ ਇਕ ਆਖ੍ਹਰ॥ ਕਿਨਕਾ ਏਕ ਜਿਸੁ ਜੀਅ ਬਸਾਵੈ॥ ਤਾ ਕੀ ਮਹਿਮਾ ਗਨੀ ਨ ਆਵੈ॥ ਕਾਖੀ ਏਕੈ ਦਰਸ ਤੁਹਾਰੋ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਉਨ ਸੰਗਿ ਮੋਹਿ ਉਧਾਰੋ॥  1॥

ਸੁਖਮਨੀ ਸੁਖ ਅੰਮ੍ਰਿਤ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਨਾਮੁ॥ ਭਗਤ ਜਨਾ ਕੈ ਮਨਿ ਬਿਸ੍ਰਾਮੁ॥ ਰਹਾਉ॥

Ashtpudi: Simroun simer(i) simer(i) sookh(u) paavoun. Kul(i) klaysh tun maah(i) mitaavoun. Simeroun jaas(u) Bisumbhur Aikaiy. Naam juput agunut anaykaiy. Bayd Puran Simrit(i) soodhaakhur.  Keenay Ram Naam ikk Aakhur.  Kinka aik jis(u) jea busaavaiy. Ta ki mehma guni na aavaiy.  Kaankhi aikaiy durs Toohaaro.  Nanak oon sung(i) moh(i) oodhaaro.    1.

Sukhmani, sookh Amrit Prubh Naam(u).  Bhugut juna kaiy mun(i) bisraam.  Ruhaou. 

Word Meaning: Simroun = I recite.  Kul(i) Klaysh = internal conflicts (struggle against desires).  Jaas(u) = praise.    Bisumbhur = the provider for the universe.  Soodhaakhur = the holy word.   Kinka = grain; a small amount.   Mehma = praise.  Kaankhi = wish.1.   

Sukhmani = the source of all comforts.     Bisraam = peace of mind; bliss.        Pause.

MEANING:  I recite Waheguru’s Naam and enjoy comfort (blissfulness). Reciting Waheguru’s Naam eradicates my internal conflicts (the fight against desires).  I sing praise only of (Waheguru), which is the provider for the whole universe. (But I am not the only one), countless people recite His Naam. If there is a holy word in the Vedas, the Puranas, and the Simritis, it is because of the one word – Waheguru’s Naam. Even if a small amount of Waheguru’s Naam begins to dwell in a devotee’s heart, he becomes praise-worthy beyond measurement.  I, Nanak, have only one desire – to have a glimpse of Yours, oh Waheguru.  Kindly salvage me along with Your holy devotees.     1.   

Waheguru’s Naam is the real elixir or ambrosia (the liquid of immortality), and is the source of all comforts. It produces bliss in the minds of the devotees.   Pause.

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਗਰਭਿ ਨ ਬਸੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਦੂਖੁ ਜਮੁ ਨਸੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕਾਲੁ ਪਰਹਰੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਦੁਸਮਨੁ ਟਰੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕਛੁ ਬਿਘਨੁ ਨ ਲਾਗੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਅਨਦਿਨੁ ਜਾਗੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਭਉ ਨ ਬਿਆਪੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਦੁਖੁ ਨ ਸੰਤਾਪੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਾ ਸਿਮਰਨੁ ਸਾਧ ਕੈ ਸੰਗਿ॥ ਸਰਬ ਨਿਧਾਨ ਨਾਨਕ ਹਰਿ ਰੰਗਿ॥  2॥

Prubh kaiy simrin(i) gerbh(i) na busaiy. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) dookh Jum(u) nusaiy. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) kaal(u) perhuraiy. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) dooshmun(u) turaiy. Prubh simrin kuchh(u) bighun(u) na laagaiy. Prubh kaiy simrin undin(u) jaagaiy. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) bhuou na biyaapaiy. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) dookh na suntaapaiy. Prubh ka simrin(u) Sadh kaiy sung(i). Serb nidhaan Nanak Hur(i) rung(i).    2.

Word Meaning: Gerbh(i) = in the womb.  Jum(u) = the Angel of death.  Kaal(u) = (fear of) death.  Purhuraiy = goes away; is dispelled.    Turaiy = avert; pass by without causing any harm.  Bighun(u) = obstruction; hindrance.    Suntaapaiy = tortures.  Nidhaan = treasures.  Rung(i) = in love.   

MEANING: An individual who remembers Waheguru never goes into the womb (is not reborn again).  The worry of any possible punishment by the Angel of death and the fear of death are dispelled when a person recites Waheguru’s Naam.  The enemies (the five demons, lust, etc.) cannot cause any harm when a man recites Waheguru’s Naam. There is no hindrance (by the sensual desires) in the life of a devotee because he is alert against their attacks, day and night.  No fear can impact a devotee who recites Waheguru’s Naam, and no worry tortures him (he becomes worry-free).  Recitation of Waheguru’s Naam is a gift that one receives in the company (shelter) of the Guru.  When a person recites Waheguru’s Naam with affection and dedication, he feels as if he has acquired all the nine mythical treasures, oh Nanak.      2.

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਰਿਧਿ ਸਿਧਿ ਨਉ ਨਿਧਿ। ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਗਿਆਨੁ ਧਿਆਨੁ ਤਤੁ ਬੁਧਿ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਜਪ ਤਪ ਪੂਜਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਬਿਨਸੈ ਦੂਜਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਤੀਰਥ ਇਸਨਾਨੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨ ਦਰਗਹ ਮਾਨੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਹੋਇ ਸੁ ਭਲਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਸੁਫਲ ਫਲਾ॥ ਸੇ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਜਿਨ ਆਪਿ ਸਿਮਰਾਏ॥

(ਅੰਗ 263)॥     ਨਾਨਕ ਤਾ ਕੈ ਲਾਗਉ ਪਾਏ॥ 3॥

Prubh kaiy simrin(i) ridh(i) sidh(i) nuou nidh(i). Prubh kaiy simrin(i) Giyan(u) dhiyaan(u) Tut(u) boodh(i). Prubh kaiy simrin(i) jup tup pooja. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) binsaiy dooja.  Prubh kaiy simrin(i) teeruth ishnaani. Prubh kaiy simrin Durgeh maani. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) hoyay su bhula. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) sooful fula. Say simreh(i) jin Aap(i) simraayay. 

(PAGE 263):         Nanak ta kaiy laagoun paayay. 

Word Meaning:   Ridh(i) Sidh(i)  = supernatural powers.   Nuou Nidh(i) = the nine mythical treasures of god Kuber.   Giyaan(u) = Divine Knowledge.  Dhiyaan = concentration.     Tut(u) = essence; the origin.   Boodh(i) = wisdom.   Binsaiy = vanishes.    Dooja = the other (Maya).   Bhula = good.   Sooful Fula = wished reward. 

MEANING:  When a man recites Waheguru’s Naam, he feels as if he has acquired all the supernatural powers, and the nine mythical treasures (they become unimportant to him); he does acquire the Divine Knowledge (gets connected with Waheguru), the ability to concentrate for long times, and the wisdom to realize the origin of everything (Waheguru).  When a person recites Waheguru’s Naam, he does not need any ritual, such as reciting mantras, self-affliction, or the worship of a deity, and his love for Maya vanishes.   Such a devotee does not need any pilgrimage.  He is honoured in Waheguru’s Court.  Anything that happens to the individual who recites Waheguru’s Naam is for his good, and he gets all his wishes rewarded (fulfilled).  However, only those people recite Waheguru’s Naam whom He wants to do so.  I wish to drop at the feet of such lucky people (serve them diligently), oh Nanak.   3.

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਾ ਸਿਮਰਨੁ ਸਭ ਤੇ ਊਚਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਉਧਰੇ ਮੂਚਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਤ੍ਰਿਸਨਾ ਬੁਝੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਸਭੁ ਕਿਛੁ ਸੁਝੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਨਾਹੀ ਜਮ ਤ੍ਰਾਸਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਪੁਰਨ ਆਸਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਮਨ ਕੀ ਮਲੁ ਜਾਇ॥ ਅੰਮ੍ਰਿਤ ਨਾਮੁ ਰਿਦ ਮਾਹਿ ਸਮਾਇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਜੀ ਬਸਹਿ ਸਾਧ ਕੀ ਰਸਨਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਨ ਕਾ ਦਾਸਨਿ ਦਸਨਾ॥ 4॥

Prubh ka simrun(u) subh tay oocha. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) oodhray moocha.  Prub kaiy simrin(i) trishna boojhaiy. Prubh kaiy simrun(i) subh(u) kichh(u) soojhaiy.  Prubh kaiy simrin(i) naahi Jum traasa. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) poorun aasa. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) mun kee mul(u) jaayay.  Amrit Naam(u) rid maah(i) smaayay.  Prubh ji buseh(i) Sadh kee rusna. Nanak jun ka daasun(i) dusna.   4.

Word Meaning:  Moocha = very many; countless.  Trishna = fire of desires.  Soojhaiy = can think of; is able to find a solution.   Traasa = fear.   Mul(u) = filth.   

MEANING: Remembering Waheguru (keeping him in mind) is the highest spiritual worship, and countless people have found salvation by reciting Waheguru’s Naam.   Man’s fire of his desires is put out by the recitation of Waheguru’s Naam, and he achieves the ability to find a solution to all his problems (how to break the bonds of Maya).   The Angel of Death can strike no fear in the mind of the devotee, and all his wishes are fulfilled. Recitation of Waheguru’s Naam washes away the filth of one’s mind, and the Naam-Amrit takes root in the devotee’s heart.  Waheguru dwells on the tongue of the Guru (Guru’s tongue always recites His Naam), and I am the slave of Waheguru’s slaves (devotees), oh Nanak.   4.

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਧਨਵੰਤੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਪਤਿਵੰਤੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਜਨ ਪਰਵਾਨ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਪੁਰਖ ਪ੍ਰਧਾਨ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸਿ ਬੇਮੁਹਤਾਜੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸਿ ਸਰਬ ਕੇ ਰਾਜੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਸੁਖਵਾਸੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸਦਾ ਅਬਿਨਾਸੀ॥ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਤੇ ਲਾਗੇ ਜਿਨ ਆਪਿ ਦਇਆਲਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਨ ਕੀ ਮੰਗੈ ਰਵਾਲਾ॥5॥

Prubh kuou simreh(i) say dunvuntay. Prubh kuou simreh(i) say putivuntay. Prubh kuou simreh(i) say jun pervaan. Prubh kuou simreh(i) say purakh perdhaan. Prubh kuou simreh(i) s(i) baymoohtaajay. Prubh kuou simreh(i) s(i) serb kay raajay. Prubh kuou simreh(i) say sookhvaasi.  Prubh kuou simreh(i) suda abinaashi. Simrin(i) tay laagay jin Aap(i) duyaala. Nanak jun kee mungaiy ruvaala.  5.

Word Meaning: Dhunvuntay = wealthy.   Putivuntay = honourable.   Pervaan = approved.     Baymoohtaajay = not dependent on anyone.   Abinaashi = immortal.   Ruvaala = dust of the feet.

MEANING: The individuals who recite Waheguru’s Naam are truly wealthy and are honourable people. They are approved (honoured) in Waheguru’s Court and are the (spiritual) leaders of society.  They are not dependent on anyone; on the contrary, others treat them like a king (they earn respect in society).  The individuals who recite Waheguru’s Naam are always blissful and become immortal. However, only those recite Waheguru’s Naam who are kindly motivated to do so.  I beg for the dust of their feet (wish to serve them), oh Nanak.     5. 

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਪਰਉਪਕਾਰੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਤਿਨ ਸਦ ਬਲਿਹਾਰੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਮੁਖ ਸੁਹਾਵੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਤਿਨ ਸੂਖਿ ਬਿਹਾਵੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਤਿਨ ਆਤਮੁ ਜੀਤਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਤਿਨ ਨਿਰਮਲ ਰੀਤਾ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਤਿਨ ਅਨਦ ਘਨੇਰੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਬਸਹਿ ਹਰਿ ਨੇਰੇ॥ ਸੰਤ ਕ੍ਰਿਪਾ ਤੇ ਅਨਦਿਨੁ ਜਾਗਿ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਸਿਮਰਨੁ ਪੂਰੈ ਭਾਗਿ॥ 6॥

Prubh kuou simreh(i) say pur-oopkaari.  Prubh kuou simreh(i) tin sud bulihaari.  Prubh kuou simreh(i) say mookh soohaavay. Prubh kuou simreh(i) tin sookh(i) bihaavaiy. Prubh kuou simreh(i) tin aatum jeeta. Prubh kuou simreh(i) tin nirmul reeta.  Prubh kuou simreh(i) tin anund ghunayray.  Prubh kuou simreh(i) buseh(i) Hur(i) nayray.  Sunt kirpa tay undin(u) jaag(i).  Nanak simrin(u) pooraiy bhaag(i).   6.

Word Meaning:  Peroopkaari = altruist; benefactor; help the needy.    Soohaavay = pleasant; dignified.   Bihaavaiy = is spent.     Aatum(u) = soul; mind.   Reeta = way of life.   Anund = joys.   Ghunayray = many; much.     Jaag(i) = stay alert. 

MEANING:  Those who recite Waheguru’s Naam are always helpful to the needy; I wish to lay down my life for them.  Those who recite Waheguru’s Naam are very pleasant to talk to; (and because of this) their life is spent in comfort (no worries).  The devotees of Waheguru have full control of their minds and have a very holy lifestyle. Such devotees have a great degree of blissfulness because they are so close to Waheguru.  Only individuals with great fortune recite Waheguru’s Naam with Guru’s grace, and stay alert against the attacks of Maya, day and night, oh Nanak.  6.

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕਾਰਜ ਪੂਰੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕਬਹੁ ਨ ਝੂਰੇ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਹਰਿ ਗੁਨ ਬਾਨੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਸਹਜਿ ਸਮਾਨੀ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਨਿਹਚਲ ਆਸਨੁ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕਮਲ ਬਿਗਾਸਨੁ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੈ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਅਨਹਦ ਝੁਨਕਾਰ॥ ਸੁਖ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਸਿਮਰਨ ਕਾ ਅੰਤੁ ਨ ਪਾਰ॥ ਸਿਮਰਹਿ ਸੇ ਜਨ ਜਿਨ ਕਉ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਮਇਆ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤਿਨ ਜਨ ਸਰਨੀ ਪਇਆ॥7 ॥

Prubh kaiy simrin(i) kaaruj pooray. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) kubhoun na jhooraiy  Prubh kaiy simrin Hur(i) goon baani.  Prubh kaiy simrin(i) sehj(i) smaani.  Prubh kaiy simrin(i) nihchul aasun(u).  Prubh kaiy simrin(i) kumul bigaasun. Prubh kaiy simrin(i) unhud jhoonkaar. Sookh Prubh simrin ka unt(u) na paar. Simreh(i) say jun jin kuou Prubh muyya. Nanak tin jun sherni puyya.   7.

Word Meaning:   Kaaruj = objective.  Pooray = achieved.   Jhooray = lament; complain.  Sehj(i) = in state of bliss (above the effects of Maya).  Nihchul  Aasun(u) = stability of mind (peaceful).   Kumul = lotus; heart.   Bigaasun = blooming.   Unhud Jhoonkaar = soft auto-playing Divine Music.   Muyya = kindness.  

MEANING:   All the objectives of a devotee, who recites Waheguru’s Naam, are achieved successfully, and he never has to lament. Singing Waheguru’s praise becomes the normal conversation of such a devotee; he attains and stays in the state of bliss above the effects of Maya.  Such a devotee has stability of his mind (which never wavers), and the lotus of his heart blooms all the time.  With the recitation of Waheguru’s Naam, the devotee feels as if he is listening to the soft auto-playing Divine Music which the yogis are believed to hear in their meditation, and there is no limit to the comfort provided by the recitation of the Naam.  Only those recite Waheguru’s Naam whom He is kind to. I wish to drop at the feet of such devotees (seek their shelter), oh Nanak.   7.

ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨ ਕਰਿ ਭਗਤ ਪ੍ਰਗਟਾਏ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਲਗ ਬੇਦ ਉਪਾਏ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਭਏ ਸਿਧ ਜਤੀ ਦਾਤੇ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਨੀਚ ਚਹੁੰ ਕੁੰਟ ਜਾਤੇ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਧਾਰੀ ਸਭ ਧਰਨਾ॥ ਸਿਮਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਕਾਰਨ ਕਰਨਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਕੀਓ ਸਗਲ ਅਕਾਰਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਮਹਿ ਆਪਿ ਨਿਰੰਕਾਰਾ॥ ਕਰਿ ਕਿਰਪਾ ਜਿਸੁ ਆਪਿ ਬੁਝਾਇਆ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਹਰਿ ਸਿਮਰਨਿ ਤਿਨਿ ਪਾਇਆ॥ 8॥1॥

Hur(i) simrin kur(i) bhugut pergtaayay. Hur(i) simrin lug Bayd oopaayay.  Hur(i) simrin(i) bhuyyay Sidh jutee daatay. Hur(i) simrin(i) neech chuhoun koont jaatay. Hur(i) simrin(i) dhaari subh dherna. Simer(i) simer(i) Hur(i) kaarun kerna. Hur(i) simrin(i) keeyo sugul akaara. Hur(i) simrin meh(i) Aap(i) Nirunkaara.  Kur(i) kirpa jis(u) Aap(i) boojhaaya. Nanak Gurmukh(i) Hur(i) simrin(i) tin(i) paaya.  8.

Word Meaning: Pergtaayay = made famous.  Jutee = celibates.   Daatay = donors; philanthropists.   Neech = the lowly (untouchables).   Chuhoun Koont = in all the four directions.   Dhaari = supports.   Dherna = earth.   Kaarun Kerna = the Creator.   Boojhaaya = made to understand. 

MEANING: The Bhagats (fully dedicated devotees of Waheguru) became famous because of their recitation of Waheguru’s Naam.  The Vedas were written by their authors when prompted to do so, while remembering Waheguru (everything is controlled by Waheguru, who makes individuals do good or bad deeds based on their previous deeds).  Individuals become Sidhs, celibates, and altruists when prompted by Waheguru when they recite His Naam.  It was because of their recitation of Waheguru’s Naam that the individuals belonging to low castes came to be well known in the world.  It is by recitation of Waheguru’s Naam that the devotee realizes that the earth is being supported by Waheguru (and not by some ox or Hercules); also, the devotee realizes that it is Waheguru who is the creator of the universe, and of all the universal bodies.   Waheguru is present where His Naam is being recited.  But only that individual recites Waheguru’s Naam in the shelter of the Guru, who is given the wisdom (is motivated) by Waheguru, oh Nanak.   8.1. 

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਦੀਨ ਦਰਦ ਦੁਖ ਭੰਜਨਾ ਘਟਿ ਘਟਿ ਨਾਥ ਅਨਾਥ॥ ਸਰਣਿ ਤੁਮ੍ਹਾਰੀ ਆਇਓ ਨਾਨਕ ਕੇ

ਪ੍ਰਭ ਸਾਥ॥ 2॥

Sloak(u): Deen durd dookh-bhunjna, ghut(i), ghut(i) Naath anaath.  Shern Toomhaari aayo, Nanak kay Prubh saath.  2.

Word Meaning:   Deen = helpless; poor; humble.   Bhunjuna = destroyer.   Ghut(i) = in heart.  Naath = Lord; Guardian.   Anaath = (spiritually) orphans; support-less.   Saath = with; save        2.   

MEANING:  You are the destroyer of all pain and suffering of the humble, and are present in every heart, oh, the Guardian of the support-less, Waheguru. I, too, have come to Your shelter, please, be with me (save me), oh Lord.   

(ਅੰਗ 264)॥   ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਜਹ ਮਾਤ ਪਿਤਾ ਸੁਤ ਮੀਤ ਨ ਭਾਈ॥ ਮਨ ਊਹਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਤੇਰੈ ਸੰਗਿ ਸਹਾਈ॥ ਜਹ ਮਹਾ ਭਇਆਨ ਦੂਤ ਜਮ ਦਲੈ॥ ਤਹ ਕੇਵਲ ਨਾਮੁ ਸੰਗਿ ਤੇਰੈ ਚਲੈ॥ ਜਹ ਮੁਸਕਲ ਹੋਵੈ ਅਤਿ ਭਾਰੀ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੋ ਨਾਮੁ ਖਿਨ ਮਾਹਿ ਉਧਾਰੀ॥ ਅਨਿਕ ਪੁਨਹਚਰਨ ਕਰਤ ਨਹੀ ਤਰੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੋ ਨਾਮੁ ਕੋਟਿ ਪਾਪ ਪਰਹਰੈ॥ ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਹੁ ਮਨ ਮੇਰੇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪਾਵਹੁ ਸੂਖ ਘਨੇਰੇ॥  1॥

(PAGE 264): Ashtpudi:  Jeh maat pita, soot, meet, na bhaayee.  Mun oohaan Naam(u) tayraiy sung(i) suhaayee.  Jeh muha bhuyaan doot Jum dulaiy.  Teh kayvul Naam(u) sung(i) tayraiy chulaiy.  Jeh mooshkil hovaiy ut(i) bhaari.  Hur(i) ko Naam(u) khin maah(i) oodhaari. Anik punehchurn kert nuhee turaiy. Hur(i) ko Naam(u) koat(i) paap purhuraiy.  Gurmukh(i) Naam(u) jup-hou mun mayray.  Nanak paavhou sookh ghunayray.   1.

Word Meaning:  Soot = sons.  Suhaayee = helpful.   Bhuyaan = terrifying.  Doot Jum Dulaiy = army of the assistants of the angel of death.    Khin = moment.   Oodhaari = protect; get out of trouble.    Punehchurn = rituals to have sins forgiven; penitence.  Turaiy = salvaged.   Purhuraiy = dispels.      Ghunayray = very many.     1.

MEANING: Where there is no one like your mother, father, son, friend, or brother to help you, Waheguru’s Naam is always there to help you, oh man.  Where there is a terrifying army of the Angel of Death, Waheguru’s Naam is again there to protect you.  When you are in big trouble, Waheguru’s Naam gets you out of that trouble in a moment. Countless rituals of penitence cannot wash away the sins of an individual, but, Waheguru’s Naam eradicates millions of sins.   Recite Waheguru’s Naam in the shelter of the Guru, and enjoy all sorts of comforts, oh my mind.     1. 

ਸਗਲ ਸ੍ਰਿਸਟਿ ਕੋ ਰਾਜਾ ਦੁਖੀਆ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਹੋਇ ਸੁਖੀਆ॥ ਲਾਖ ਕਰੋਰੀ ਬੰਧੁ ਨ ਪਰੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਨਿਸਤਰੈ॥ ਅਨਿਕ ਮਾਇਆ ਰੰਗ ਤਿਖ ਨ ਬੁਝਾਵੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਅਘਾਵੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਮਾਰਗ ਇਹੁ ਜਾਤ ਇਕੇਲਾ॥ ਤਹ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਸੰਗਿ ਹੋਤ ਸੁਹੇਲਾ॥ ਐਸਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਮਨ ਸਦਾ ਧਿਆਈਐ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਪਰਮ ਗਤਿ ਪਾਈਐ॥  2॥

Sugul srisht(i) ko raja dookhiya. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) juput hoyay sookhiya.  Laakh krorie, bundh(u) na puraiy. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) juput nisturaiy.  Anik Maya rung tikh na boojhaavaiy. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) juput aghaavaiy.  Jih maarug ehou jaat ikayla. Teh Hur(i) ka Naam(u) sung(i) hoat soohayla.  Aiysa Naam(u) mun suda dhiyaayiyaiy. Nanak Gurmukh(i) perm gut(i) paayiyaiy.    2.

Word Meaning:   Srisht(i) = world.   Laakh Krorie = millions.  Bundh(u) = contain; put a stop.  Nisturaiy = is salvaged.    Tikh = thirst for Maya.  Aghaavaiy = is contented.    Soohayla = comforting.   Perm gut(i) = highest spiritual level.   

MEANING:  Even the emperor of the whole world suffers from many worries, but a devotee, who recites Waheguru’s Naam, is always blissful.  Millions of rupees (countless money) do not put a stop to the further desires of a man (and he is further shackled by the bonds of Maya); but a devotee gets freedom from the bonds of Maya by worshipping Waheguru.  Countless pleasures of Maya do not quench the thirst of the man for Maya, but he is fully contented by reciting Waheguru’s Naam.  The man has to go alone on the journey to the Angel of death, but a devotee is not alone as Waheguru’s comforting Naam is with him.  Recite such Naam of Waheguru in the shelter of the Guru, which enables you to achieve the highest spiritual state, oh my (Nanak’s) mind.     2.

ਛੂਟਤ ਨਹੀ ਕੋਟਿ ਲਖ ਬਾਹੀ॥ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਤਾ ਪਾਰਿ ਪਰਾਹੀ॥ ਅਨਿਕ ਬਿਘਨ ਜਹ ਆਇ ਸੰਘਾਰੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਤਤਕਾਲ ਉਧਾਰੈ॥ ਅਨਿਕ ਜੋਨਿ ਜਨਮੈ ਮਰਿ ਜਾਮ॥ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਪਾਵੈ ਬਿਸ੍ਰਾਮ॥ ਹਉ ਮੈਲਾ ਮਲੁ ਕਬਹੁ ਨ ਧੋਵੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਕੋਟਿ ਪਾਪ ਖੋਵੈ॥ ਐਸਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਹੁ ਮਨ ਰੰਗਿ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪਾਈਐ ਸਾਧ ਕੈ ਸੰਗਿ॥ 3॥

Chhootut nuhee koat(i) lukh baahi.  Naam(u) juput ta, paar(i) puraahi.  Anik bighun jeh aayay sunghaaraiy. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) tutkaal(u) oodhaaraiy.  Anik joan(i) junmaiy mur(i) jaam. Naam(u) juput paavaiy bisraam.  Huoun maiyla, mul(u) kubhoun na dhovaiy. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) koat(i) paap khovaiy. Aiysa Naam(u) jup-hou mun rung(i). Nanak paayiyaiy Sadh kaiy sung(i).  3.

Word Meaning:   Baahi = arms (army).   Paar(i) Puraahi = is ferried across.   Bighun = obstacles; hindrances.    Sunghaaraiy = killed; is incapacitated. Tutkaal = right away.    Bisraam = rest; peace.  Huoun = selfishness and ego.   Rung(i) = with love. 

MEANING:  The man does not get freedom from the bonds of Maya even if he has command of an army of millions. However, by reciting Waheguru’s Naam, he easily finds salvation (swims across the sea of Maya). When a man is incapacitated by countless hindrances, he is pulled out of trouble right away by Waheguru’s Naam.  Normally, the man takes birth in countless different life forms and dies, but he finds rest (his rebirth cycle is broken) if he recites Waheguru’s Naam. The man is filthy with haumein, and he never cleanses his filth (continues to live with haumein in different lives), but Waheguru’s Naam washes away the filth of millions of sins.  So, recite such a useful Naam of Waheguru with love, oh my mind. Waheguru’s Naam is acquired in the company (shelter of the Guru), oh Nanak.    3.   

ਜਿਹ ਮਾਰਗ ਕੇ ਗਨੇ ਜਾਇ ਨ ਕੋਸਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਊਹਾ ਸੰਗਿ ਤੋਸਾ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪੈਡੈ ਮਹਾ ਅੰਧ ਗੁਬਾਰਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਸੰਗਿ ਉਜਿਆਰਾ॥ ਜਹਾ ਪੰਥਿ ਤੇਰਾ ਕੋ ਨ ਸਿਞਾਨੂ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਤਹ ਨਾਲਿ ਪਛਾਨੂ॥ ਜਹ ਮਹਾ ਭਇਆਨ ਤਪਤਿ ਬਹੁ ਘਾਮ॥ ਤਹ ਹਰਿ ਕੇ ਨਾਮ ਕੀ ਤੁਮ ਊਪਰਿ ਛਾਮ॥ ਜਹਾ ਤ੍ਰਿਖਾ ਮਨ ਤੁਝੁ ਆਕਰਖੈ॥ ਤਹ ਨਾਨਕ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਅੰਮ੍ਰਿਤੁ ਬਰਖੈ॥ 4॥

Jih maarug kay gunay jaayay na koasa. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) oohaan sung(i) toasa.  Jih paiyndaiy muha undh goobaara.  Hur(i) ka Naam(u) sung(i) oojiyaara.  Juhaa Punth(u) tayra ko na sinyaanoo. Hur(i) ka Naam teh naal(i) puchhaanoo.  Jeh muha bhuyaan tupt(i) buhou ghaam.  The Hur(i) kay Naam kee toom oopur(i) chhaam.  Juhaa trikha mun toojh aakerkhaiy.  Teh Nanak Hur(i) Hur(i) Amrit(u) berkhaiy.    4.

Word Meaning:   Koasa = a unit of distance (1 koas = 2.2 km).  Toasa = journey expenses (food, drinks, clothes, etc.)   Paiyndaiy = journey.  Undh Goobaara = dark blind.    Oojiyaara = bright light.   Punth(i) = faith.   Puchhaanoo = identity.  Tupt(i) Buhou Ghaam = extreme heat.   Trikha = thirst      Aakerkhaiy = attracts; exerts its killing effect.    Berkhaiy = rains. 

NOTE: According to Hindu texts, a man’s soul takes a full year to reach his ancestors. The path is very dark, frightening, uncomfortable involving extreme heat.  The man is alone and a stranger. 

MEANING:  No one can measure the distance of the journey (to the ancestors; the naked soul is alone and without any food).  In such a difficult journey, Waheguru’s Naam serves as his journey expenses.  Where the path is blindingly dark, Waheguru’s Naam serves as the bright light.  Where nobody is familiar with your faith, Waheguru’s Naam is your identity.  Where there is intense heat along the journey to the ancestors, Waheguru’s Naam provides comforting shade.  When thirst exerts its killing effect on the soul on its journey, Waheguru’s Naam comes down as rain to quench the thirst, oh Nanak.    4.

ਭਗਤ ਜਨਾ ਕੀ ਬਰਤਨਿ ਨਾਮੁ॥ ਸੰਤ ਜਨਾ ਕੈ ਮਨਿ ਬਿਸ੍ਰਾਮੁ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਦਾਸ ਕੀ ਓਟ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੈ ਨਾਮਿ ਉਧਰੇ ਜਨ ਕੋਟਿ॥ ਹਰਿ ਜਸੁ ਕਰਤ ਸੰਤ ਦਿਨੁ ਰਾਤਿ॥ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਅਉਖਧੁ ਸਾਧ ਕਮਾਤਿ॥ ਹਰਿ ਜਨ ਕੈ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਨਿਧਾਨੁ॥ ਪਾਰਬ੍ਰਹਮਿ ਜਨ ਕੀਨੋ ਦਾਨ॥ ਮਨ ਤਨ ਰੰਗਿ ਰਤੇ ਰੰਗ ਏਕੈ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਨ ਕੈ ਬਿਰਤਿ ਬਿਬੇਕੈ॥ 5

Bhugut juna kee bertun(i) Naam(u).  Sunt juna kaiy mun(i) bisraam(u).  Hur(i) ka Naam(u) daas kee oat. Hur(i) kaiy Naam(i) oodhraiy jun koat(i).  Hur(i) jus(u) kert Sunt din(u) raat(i).  Hur(i) Hur(i) aukhudh sadh kumaat(i).  Hur(i) jun kaiy Hur(i) Naam(u) nidhaan(u). Parbraham jun keeno daan.  Mun tun rung(i) rutay rung aikaiy.  Nanak jun kaiy birt(i) bibaykaiy.    5.

Word Meaning:   Bertun(i) = routine dealings.   Bisraam(u) = peace.   Daas = devotee.   Oat = support.   Oodhray = salvaged.    Aukhudh = medication.   Kmaat(i) = acquire; accumulate as wealth.   Nidhaan(u) = treasure.   Rutay = imbued.    Birt(i) = in thoughts (mind).   Bibakaiy = Divine Knowledge.      5.

MEANING: Waheguru’s Naam becomes the routine of the devotees in their daily dealings, and they have peace (calmness) of mind (never get angry).  Waheguru’s Naam is the shelter of the devotees, and countless devotees find salvation with His Naam. The holy devotees sing Waheguru’s praise day and night, and accumulate Waheguru’s Naam as medication for all ailments (as the panacea).   Waheguru’s Naam is the treasure of wealth for the devotees, which Waheguru has bestowed on them.   Their mind and body are imbued with the love of only the “One” (Waheguru).  The devotees are gifted with the Divine Knowledge (wisdom) to distinguish between what to adopt and what to reject, oh Nanak.  5.

ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਨ ਕਉ ਮੁਕਤਿ ਜੁਗਤਿ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੈ ਨਾਮਿ ਜਨ ਕਉ ਤ੍ਰਿਪਤਿ ਭੁਗਤਿ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਨ ਕਾ ਰੂਪ ਰੰਗੁ॥ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਕਬ ਪਰੈ ਨ ਭੰਗੁ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਨ ਕੀ ਵਡਿਆਈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੈ ਨਾਮਿ ਜਨ ਸੋਭਾ ਪਾਈ॥

(ਅੰਗ 265)॥   ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਨ ਕਉ ਭੋਗੁ ਜੋਗੁ॥ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਕਛੁ ਨਾਹਿ ਬਿਓਗੁ॥ ਜਨੁ ਰਾਤਾ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮ ਕੀ ਸੇਵਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪੂਜੈ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਦੇਵਾ॥ 6॥

Hur(i) ka Naam(u) jun kuou mookut(i) joogut(i).  Hur(i) kaiy Naam(i) jun kuou triput(i) bhoogut(i).  Hur(i) ka Naam(u) jun ka roop rung(u).  Hur(i) Naam juput, kub puraiy na bhung(u). Hur(i) ka Naam jun kee vudiyaayi. Hur(i) kaiy Naam(i) jun sobha payee.

(PAGE 265):      Hur(i) ka Naam(u) jun kuou bhoag(u) joag(u). Hur(i) Naam(u) juput kuchh(u) naah(i) biyoag(u).  Jun(u) raata Hur(i) Naam kee sayva. Nanak poojaiy Hur(i) Hur(i) Dayva.   6.

Word Meaning: Mookut(i) = freedom from Maya.  Joogut(i) = technique.   Triput(i) = contentment.    Bhoogut(i) = food.    Bhung(u) = break down; obstacles.   Sobha = praise; fame.   Bhoag(u) = wealth; food.    Joag(u) = the path to union with God.    Biyoag(u) = separation.    Raata = imbued.    Dayva = god; enlightening 

MEANING: (Waheguru’s devotee does not need to put in any special effort to achieve freedom from Maya, or to follow some special way of life), Waheguru’s Naam itself is the technique for emancipation.  Waheguru’s Naam serves as the (spiritual) food for the devotee's contentment. Similarly, a devotee appears dignified with Waheguru’s Naam, which enhances his good looks and attractiveness. With recitation of Waheguru’s Naam, a devotee experiences no obstacle to acquire the state of blissfulness.   Waheguru’s Naam serves as the source of respect for the devotee, and he earns praise.  Waheguru’s Naam is the spiritual food for the devotee, and he does not need any special yoga technique to connect with God.  With recitation of Waheguru’s Naam, one suffers no separation from Him. The devotee is imbued with his love for Waheguru’s Naam, which is his service to, and worship of, the enlightening God, oh Nanak.     6. 

ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਜਨ ਕੈ ਮਾਲੁ ਖਜੀਨਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਧਨੁ ਜਨ ਕਉ ਆਪਿ ਪ੍ਰਭਿ ਦੀਨਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਜਨ ਕੈ ਓਟਿ ਸਤਾਣੀ॥ ਹਰਿ ਪ੍ਰਤਾਪਿ ਜਨ ਅਵਰ ਨ ਜਾਣੀ॥ ਓਤਿ ਪੋਤਿ ਜਨ ਹਰਿ ਰਸਿ ਰਾਤੇ॥ ਸੁੰਨ ਸਮਾਧਿ ਨਾਮ ਰਸਿ ਮਾਤੇ॥ ਆਠ ਪਹਰ ਜਨੁ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਜਪੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਭਗਤੁ ਪ੍ਰਗਟ ਨਹੀ ਛਪੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੀ ਭਗਤਿ ਮੁਕਤਿ ਬਹੁ ਕਰੇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਨ ਸੰਗਿ ਕੇਤੇ ਤਰੇ॥ 7॥

Hur(i) Hur(i) jun kaiy maal(u) khujeena.  Hur(i) dhun(u) jun kuou aap(i) Prubh(i) deena.  Hur(i) Hur(i) jun kaiy oat(i) staani. Hur(i) pertaap(i) jun uver na jaani. Oat(i) poat(i) jun Hur(i) rus(i) maatay. Soonn smaadh(i) Naam rus(i) maatay. Aatth pehr jun(u) Hur(i) Hur(i) jupaiy. Hur(i) ka bhugut(u) pergut, nuhee chhupaiy.  Hur(i) kee bhugut(i) mookut(i) buhou kuray.  Nanak jun sung(i) kaytay turay.     7. 

Word Meaning: Maal(u) = wealth.    Khujeena = treasure.   Oat(i) Staani = powerful support.   Pertaap(i) = (with) magnificence.   Jaani = recognize; acknowledge.    Oat(i) Poat(i) = cross threads in a fabric.    Raatay = imbued.   Soonn = the state where no thoughts about Maya enter the mind.   Smaadh(i) = meditation.   Maatay = intoxicated.   Aatth Pehr = 24 hours.   Pergut = clearly visible; well known.      7.

MEANING: Waheguru’s Naam is the treasure of wealth for the devotee, which has been given to him by Waheguru Himself.  Waheguru’s Naam is a powerful shelter for the devotee, and with Waheguru’s magnificence, the devotee does not recognize (care for) anybody else.   Waheguru’s devotees are imbued with the sweetness of Waheguru and are interwoven with Him like the cross threads of a fabric.  During their meditation, the devotees are so intoxicated with the sweet Naam that Maya cannot impact them in any way.  Waheguru’s devotee recites His Naam all the time, and becomes well known in the world even if he tries hard to stay unknown.  Service to God cuts the bonds of Maya of countless individuals, and many more people find salvation in their company, oh Nanak.    7.

ਪਾਰਜਾਤੁ ਇਹੁ ਹਰਿ ਕੋ ਨਾਮੁ॥ ਕਾਮਧੇਨ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਗੁਣ ਗਾਮ॥ ਸਭ ਤੇ ਊਤਮ ਹਰਿ ਕੀ ਕਥਾ॥ ਨਾਮੁ ਸੁਨਤ ਦਰਦ ਦੁਖ ਲਥਾ॥ ਨਾਮ ਕੀ ਮਹਿਮਾ ਸੰਤ ਰਿਦ ਵਸੈ॥ ਸੰਤ ਪ੍ਰਤਾਪਿ ਦੁਰਤੁ ਸਭੁ ਨਸੈ॥ ਸੰਤ ਕਾ ਸੰਗੁ ਵਡਭਾਗੀ ਪਾਈਐ॥ ਸੰਤ ਕੀ ਸੇਵਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਧਿਆਈਐ॥ ਨਾਮੁ ਤੁਲਿ ਕਛੁ ਅਵਰੁ ਨ ਹੋਇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਪਾਵੈ ਜਨੁ ਕੋਇ॥ 8॥2॥

Paarjaat(u) ehou Hur(i) ko Naam(u). Kaamdhayn Hur(i) Hur(i) goon gaam.  Subh tay ootum Hur(i) kee kutha.  Naam(u) soonut durd dookh lutha. Naam kee mehma Sunt rid vusaiy. Sunt pertaap(i) doort(u) subh(u) nusaiy. Sunt ka sung(u) vudbhaagee paayiyaiy. Sunt kee sayva, Naam(u) dhiyaayiyaiy.  Naam(u) tool(i) kuchh(u) uver(u) na hoyay.  Nanak Gurmukh(i) Naam(u) paavaiy jun(u) koyay.   8.2.

Word Meaning:   Paarjaat(u) = a mythical tree in paradise which is believed to have the power to grant all wishes.   Kamdhenu = a mythical cow which has similar powers as Paarjaat(u).  Kaam = wish (fulfilling). Dhayn = cow.  Both, Paarjaat, and Kamdhenu were among the 14 jewels that were retrieved when the Kheer ocean was churned jointly by the gods and the demons.   Ootum = supreme.   Kutha = story; narrative.   Mehma = importance; value.  Pertaap(i) = magnificence.   Doort(u) = sins.   Tool(i) = equal.       8.

MEANING: Waheguru’s Naam is the Parijat tree, and singing His praise is the Kamdhenu for a devotee (he does not need their support). Describing Waheguru’s virtues (singing His praise) is the supreme (best) way (to connect with) God.  Listening to Waheguru’s Naam shakes off all the pain and misery.   The holy saints harbour the invaluable Naam of Waheguru in their hearts, and with the magnificence of the Guru, all their sins disappear.  However, it is only by great luck that one gets to be in the company of the Guru. Recitation of Waheguru’s Naam is the service to the Guru.  There is nothing to rival Waheguru’s Naam, but some rare person gets to acquire Naam in the shelter of the Guru, oh Nanak.    8.2.

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਬਹੁ ਸਾਸਤ੍ਰ ਬਹੁ ਸਿਮ੍ਰਿਤੀ ਪੇਖੇ ਸਰਬ ਢਢੋਲਿ॥ ਪੂਜਸਿ ਨਾਹੀ ਹਰਿ ਹਰੇ ਨਾਨਕ ਨਾਮੁ ਅਮੋਲ॥ 1॥

Sloak(u): Buhou Shastar buhou Simriti paykhay serb dhundhoal(i). Poojus(i) naahi Hur(i) Huray, Nanak Naam(u) amoal.   1.

Word Meaning:  Dhundhoal(I) = search.   Poojus(i) = reach; connect with.     Amoal = priceless.

MEANING:  I have searched all the Shastras and the Simritis, and found out that none of them compares with the priceless Naam of Waheguru, because none of them, except Waheguru’s Naam, can connect you with Him, oh Nanak.

ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਜਾਪ ਤਾਪ ਗਿਆਨ ਸਭਿ ਧਿਆਨ॥ ਖਟ ਸਾਸਤ੍ਰ ਸਿਮ੍ਰਿਤਿ ਵਖਿਆਨ॥ ਜੋਗ ਅਭਿਆਸ ਕਰਮ ਧਰਮ ਕਿਰਿਆ॥ ਸਗਲ ਤਿਆਗਿ ਬਨ ਮਧੇ ਫਿਰਿਆ॥ ਅਨਿਕ ਪ੍ਰਕਾਰ ਕੀਏ ਬਹੁ ਜਤਨਾ॥ ਪੁੰਨ ਦਾਨ ਹੋਮੇ ਬਹੁ ਰਤਨਾ। ਸਰੀਰੁ ਕਟਾਇ ਹੋਮੈ ਕਰਿ ਰਾਤੀ॥ ਵਰਤ ਨੇਮ ਕਰੈ ਬਹੁ ਭਾਤੀ॥ ਨਹੀ ਤੁਲ ਰਾਮ ਨਾਮ ਬੀਚਾਰ॥ ਨਾਨਕ  ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪੀਐ ਇਕ ਬਾਰ॥ 1॥

Ashtpudi: Jaap taap giyaan subh(i) dhiayaan.  Khut Shastar Simrit(i) vukhiyaan. Joag abhiyaas, kerm, dherm kiriya. Sugul tiyaag(i) bun mudhay phiriya.  Anik perkaar keeyay buhou jutna. Poonn daan homay buhou ruttna.  Sureer(u) kutaayay homaiy kur(i) raati.  Vert naym kuraiy buhou bhaanti.  Nuhee tool Ram Naam beechaar.  Nanak Gurmukh(i) Naam(u) jupiyaiy ikk baar.   1. 

Word Meaning:   Jaap = recitation of ritualistic mantras.  Taap = self-affliction to please some god.   Giyaan = knowledge; religious debates.    Khut Shastar = six Shastras (Vaiyshayshik, Nayaayay, Saankhya, Paatunjul, Meemaansa, and Vaydaant).   Vukhiyaan = narrate; explain.     Abhiyaas = practise.   Tiyaag(i) = give up; renounce.   Homay = huvun.   Ruttna = ghee.  Raati = into small pieces.   Vert = commitment to do (or not to do) something.   Naym = principles.   Tool = equal.   1.

MEANING: (People try different approaches to please gods) like, recitation of some ritualistic mantras, self-affliction, take part in religious debates, and meditate on their deity; read and explain the six Shastras and the Simritis to the people; practise yogic exercises, and perform ritualistic deeds; renounce the world and go to jungles, and try many other ways like donations (to Brahmans), perform huvuns (build ritualistic holy fires) and burn lots of ghee. They may offer their body in small pieces to the huvun fire, and may make many commitments, and put some restrictions to discipline their body and mind; but there is nothing that compares with Waheguru’s Naam, even if it is recited (with full faith) only once in the shelter of the Guru, oh Nanak.   1.

ਨਉ ਖੰਡ ਪ੍ਰਿਥਮੀ ਫਿਰੈ ਚਿਰੁ ਜੀਵੈ॥ ਮਹਾ ਉਦਾਸੁ ਤਪੀਸਰੁ ਥੀਵੈ॥ ਅਗਨਿ ਮਾਹਿ ਹੋਮਤ ਪਰਾਨ॥ ਕਨਿਕ ਅਸਵ ਹੈਵਰ ਭੂਮਿ ਦਾਨ॥ ਨਿਉਲੀ ਕਰਮ ਕਰੈ ਬਹੁ ਆਸਨ॥ ਜੈਨ ਮਾਰਗ ਸੰਜਮ ਅਤਿ ਸਾਧਨ॥ ਨਿਮਖ ਨਿਮਖ ਕਰਿ ਸਰੀਰੁ ਕਟਾਵੈ॥ ਤਉ ਭੀ ਹਉਮੈ ਮੈਲੁ ਨ ਜਾਵੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੇ ਨਾਮ ਸਮਸਰਿ ਕਛੁ ਨਾਹਿ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਗੁਰਮੁਖਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਗਤਿ ਪਾਹਿ॥ 2॥

Nuoun khund prithmi phiraiy, chir(u) jeevaiy.  Muhha oodaas(u) tupeesur(u) theevaiy. Ugun(i) maah(i) homut praan. Kunik, ashuv, haiyvur, bhoom(i) daan. Niyouli kerm, kuraiy buhou aasun. Jain marg, sunjum ut(i) saadhun.  Nimukh, nimukh, kur(i) sureer(u) kutaavaiy.  Tuou bhee haumein maiyl(u) na jaavaiy.  Hur(i) kay Naam sumsur(i) kuchh(u) naah(i).  Nanak Gurmukh(i) Naam(u) juput gut(i) paah(i).    2.

Word Meaning:  Nuoun Khund = nine regions (of the earth); all over the earth.    Chir(u) Jeevaiy = have a long life.   Oodaas = detached (from Maya).   Tupeesur = who can endure great hardships.   Theevaiy = becomes.  Homut = offer in huvun.   Kunik = gold.   Ashuv = horses.    Haiyvur = high-class elephants.   Niyouli Kerm = some physical yogic exercises to keep the body clean and healthy.  Jain Marg = the path of Jainism. Jains follow non-violence to the extreme (even cynicism) as their main objective. They keep their mouths covered so as not to breathe in any microorganisms; do not bathe for long times so as not to hurt any life in water; and so on.   Sunjum = efforts for self-control.   Saadhun = ways; means.  Nimukh = small piece.   Sumsur(i) = equal.   Gut(i) = highest spiritual state.    2.

MEANING:  If an individual leaves home and roams all over the earth to run away from the bonds of Maya, or endures severe hardships to discipline his body, and has a long life;  or offers his body to the fire of huvun; or donates horses, high quality elephants, or land to Brahmans; or practises the different exercises of yoga to keep the body clean and healthy; or follows the tough principles of non-violence of Jainism, and puts in efforts for self-control; or cuts his body into small pieces to please some god; none of these approaches washes away the filth of his ego and selfishness.  Nothing is comparable to Waheguru’s Naam for cleansing your mind of haumein (and for having union with Waheguru).  An individual who recites Waheguru’s Naam in the shelter of the Guru attains the highest spiritual state (union with God), oh Nanak.    2.

ਮਨ ਕਾਮਨਾ ਤੀਰਥ ਦੇਹ ਛੁਟੈ॥ ਗਰਬੁ ਗੁਮਾਨੁ ਨ ਮਨ ਤੇ ਹੁਟੈ॥ ਸੋਚ ਕਰੈ ਦਿਨਸੁ ਅਰੁ ਰਾਤਿ॥ ਮਨ ਕੀ ਮੈਲ ਨ ਤਨ ਤੇ ਜਾਤਿ॥ ਇਸੁ ਦੇਹੀ ਕਉ ਬਹੁ ਸਾਧਨਾ ਕਰੈ॥ਮਨ ਤੇ ਕਬਹੂ ਨ ਬਿਖਿਆ ਟਰੈ॥ ਜਲਿ ਧੋਵੈ ਬਹੁ ਦੇਹ ਅਨੀਤਿ॥ ਸੁਧ ਕਹਾ ਹੋਇ ਕਾਚੀ ਭੀਤਿ॥ ਮਨ ਹਰਿ ਕੇ ਨਾਮ ਕੀ ਮਹਿਮਾ ਊਚ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਨਾਮਿ ਉਧਰੇ  ਪਤਿਤ ਬਹੁ ਮੂਚ॥ 3॥

Mun kaamna, teeruth dayh chhootaiy.  Gerb(u) goomaan(u) na munt ay hootaiy. Soach kuraiy dinus(u) ur(u) raat(i).  Mun kee maiyl na tun tay jaat(i).  Iss(u) dayhi kuou buhou saadhna kuraiy.  Mun tay kubhoun na bikhiya turaiy.  Jul(i) dhovaiy buhou dayh aneet(i).  Soodh kuhaan hoyay kaanchi bheet(i).  Mun Hur(i) kay Naam kee mehma ooch.  Nanak Naam(i) oodhray putit buhou mooch.    3.

Word Meaning: Kaamna = wish.  Dayh = body.  Chhootaiy = become free; die. Gerb(u) = Goomaan(u) = ego.  Hootaiy = goes away.   Soach = cleanliness.    Saadhna = purify.   Bikhiya = Maya.    Turaiy = avert; leave alone.   Aneet(i) = mortal; short-lived.   Soodh = pure.   Kaachi = of mud; perishable.    Bheet(i) = wall.   Mehma = praise; glory; greatness.   Ooch = high.    Oodhray = salvaged.    Putit = immoral.       Mooch = very many.       3.

MEANING: Many men wish to breathe their last at some holy place, but their ego does not leave them alone.  They observe all sorts of ritualistic cleansing of the body, but the filth of their mind is not washed away.  The man tries many different ways to purify his body, but the appetite of his mind for Maya never goes away.  He does wash his short-lived body with water (at some holy place), but like a wall of mud, how can his mind be purified with water?  The splendour (holiness) of Waheguru’s Naam is very high, oh my mind. Countless immorals have been salvaged by His Naam, oh Nanak.3.   

ਬਹੁਤੁ ਸਿਆਣਪ ਜਮ ਕਾ ਭਉ ਬਿਆਪੈ॥

(ਅੰਗ 266)॥   ਅਨਿਕ ਜਤਨ ਕਰਿ ਤ੍ਰਿਸਨ ਨ ਧ੍ਰਾਪੈ॥ ਭੇਖ ਅਨੇਕ ਅਗਨਿ ਨਹੀ ਬੁਝੈ॥ ਕੋਟਿ ਉਪਾਵ ਦਰਗਹ ਨਹੀ ਸਿਝੈ॥ ਛੂਟਸਿ ਨਾਹੀ ਊਭ ਪਇਆਲਿ॥ ਮੋਹਿ ਬਿਆਪਹਿ ਮਾਇਆ ਜਾਲਿ॥ ਅਵਰ ਕਰਤੂਤਿ ਸਗਲੀ ਜਮੁ ਡਾਨੈ॥ ਗੋਵਿੰਦ ਭਜਨ ਬਿਨੁ ਤਿਲੁ ਨਹੀ ਮਾਨੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਤ ਦੁਖੁ ਜਾਇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਬੋਲੈ ਸਹਜਿ ਸੁਭਾਇ॥4

Buhout(u) siyaanup, Jum ka bhuou biyaapaiy.

(PAGE 266):      Anik jutun kur(i) trishun na dhraapaiy. Bhaykh anayk ugun(i) nuhee boojhaiy. Koat(i) oopaav Durgeh nuhee sijhaiy. Chhootus(i) naahi, oobh puyaal(i). Moh(i) biyaapeh(i) Maya jaal(i).  Uver kertoot(i) suglee Jum(u) daanaiy.  Govind bhujun bin(u) til(u) nuhee maanaiy.  Hur(i) ka Naam(u) juput dookh(u) jaayay. Nanak boalaiy sehj(i) soobhaayay.      4.

Word Meaning: Bhuou = fear.    Biyaapaiy = haapens; strikes.   Trishun = desires.    Dhraapaiy = contented.   Bhaykh = religious faith.  Ugun(i) = fire (of desires).  Oopaav = approaches.    Sijhaiy = succeeds.   Oobh = sky; paradise.  Puyaal(i) = underworld; hell.  Uver = others.    Kertoot(i) = deeds; rituals.   Daanaiy = punishes.   Til(u) = sesame seed; a very small amount.   Sehj(i) Soobhaayay = with love and a peaceful mind.  4

MEANING:  If a person thinks he is very wise and tries very hard to achieve all his objectives, he is never content because all his desires are never fulfilled.  Rather, he fears the Angel of Death more for his ill deeds.  The fire of his desires is never put out no matter how many different religious faiths he follows.  And, despite his countless approaches, he never succeeds in Waheguru’s Court.  He cannot escape the consequences of his deeds, and continues to be in the cycle of paradise and hell (rebirths with comforts or suffering), and the net of attachment for Maya keeps tightening on him.  Countless religious rituals fail to gain the approval of the Angel of Justice, except for Waheguru’s worship.   The suffering caused by the attachment of Maya (and therefore of rebirths) is eliminated only by Waheguru’s Naam.  So, recite Waheguru’s Naam with affection and with a peaceful mind, oh Nanak.   4.

ਚਾਰਿ ਪਦਾਰਥ ਜੇ ਕੋ ਮਾਗੈ॥ ਸਾਧ ਜਨਾ ਕੀ ਸੇਵਾ ਲਾਗੈ॥ ਜੇ ਕੋਈ ਆਪੁਨਾ ਦੂਖੁ ਮਿਟਾਵੈ॥ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਰਿਦੈ ਸਦ ਗਾਵੈ॥ ਜੇ ਕੋ ਆਪੁਨੀ ਸੋਭਾ ਲੋਰੈ॥ ਸਾਧ ਸੰਗਿ ਇਹ ਹਉਮੈ ਛੋਰੈ॥ ਜੇ ਕੋ ਜਨਮ ਮਰਣ ਤੇ ਡਰੈ॥ ਸਾਧ ਜਨਾ ਕੀ ਸਰਨੀ ਪਰੈ॥ ਜਿਸੁ ਜਨ ਕਉ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਦਰਸ ਪਿਆਸਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤਾ ਕੈ ਬਲਿ ਬਲਿ ਜਾਸਾ॥ 5॥

Chaar(i) pudaaruth jay ko maangaiy. Sadh junaa kee sayve laagaiy.  Jay koyee aapuna dookh(u) mitaavaiy. Hur(i) Hur(i) Naam(u) ridaiy sud gaavaiy.  Jay ko aapuni sobha loaraiy. Sadh sung(i) eh haumein chhoaraiy.  Jay ko junum mern tay duraiy. Sadh junaa kee sherni puraiy.  Jis jun kuou Prubh durs piyaasa. Nanak ta kaiy bul(i) bul(i) jaasa.   5.

Word Meaning: Chaar(i) Pdaaruth = the four desired products (high morality, wealth, fulfilment of wishes, and salvation).   Sobha = praise.    Loraiy = wishes.    5.

MEANING: If a person wishes for the four most popular desired products, he should serve the holy saints. If a person wishes to get rid of his troubles, he should recite Waheguru’s Naam from the depths of his heart. If a person wishes to be praised, he should give up his haumein in the company of the holy saints.  If someone is afraid of rebirths, he should seek the shelter of the holy devotees.  However, I will gladly sacrifice my life for the individual who wishes to have a sight of Waheguru, oh Nanak.    5. 

ਸਗਲ ਪੁਰਖ ਮਹਿ ਪੁਰਖੁ ਪ੍ਰਧਾਨੁ॥ ਸਾਧ ਸੰਗਿ ਜਾ ਕਾ ਮਿਟੈ ਅਭਿਮਾਨੁ॥ ਆਪਸ ਕਉ ਜੋ ਜਾਣੈ ਨੀਚਾ॥ ਸੋਊ ਗਨੀਐ ਸਭ ਤੇ ਊਚਾ॥ ਜਾ ਕਾ ਮਨੁ ਹੋਇ ਸਗਲ ਕੀ ਰੀਨਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ ਤਿਨਿ ਘਟਿ ਘਟਿ ਚੀਨਾ॥ ਮਨ ਆਪੁਨੇ ਤੈ ਬੁਰਾ ਮਿਟਾਨਾ॥ ਪੇਖੈ ਸਗਲ ਸ੍ਰਿਸਟਿ ਸਾਜਨਾ॥ ਸੂਖ ਦੂਖ ਜਨ ਸਮ ਦ੍ਰਿਸਟੇਤਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪਾਪ ਪੁੰਨ ਨਹੀ ਲੇਪਾ॥ 6॥

Sugul purakh meh purakh(u) perdhaan.  Sadh sung(i) ja ka mitaiy abhimaan(u). Aapus kuou jo jaanaiy neecha. Soyoo guniyaiy subh tay oocha.  Ja ka mun(u) hoyay sugul kee reena.  Hur(i) Hur(i) Naam(u) tin(i) ghut(i) ghut(i) cheena. Mun aapunay taiy boora mitaana. Paykhaiy sugul srisht(i) saajna. Sookh, dookh jun sum drishtayta.  Nanak, paap poonn nuhee laypa.    6.

Word Meaning:   Purakh = men. Perdhaan = leader (approved by God).  Neecha = lower.   Reena = dust (of feet).   Cheena = saw; recognized.   Boora = vices.   Srisht(i) = world.  Saajna = friends.   Jun = devotee.   Sum = equal.   Drishtayta = sees; treats.   Poonn = a deed believed to be noble.    Laypa = smeared with; affected by.       6.

MEANING:  An individual who overcomes his ego in the shelter of the Guru is the leader among everybody.  An individual who thinks himself the lowest of all is, in fact, the highest of all.  A person who is humble and respectful to others sees Waheguru in every heart. Noble deeds, or sins, have no effect on the devotee who gets rid of all his vices, treats everybody as his friend, and accepts comforts and discomforts the same 

way (is a stoic), oh Nanak.    6.

ਨਿਰਧਨ ਕਉ ਧਨੁ ਤੇਰੋ ਨਾਉ॥ ਨਿਥਾਵੇ ਕਉ ਨਾਉ ਤੇਰਾ ਥਾਉ॥ ਨਿਮਾਨੇ ਕਉ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਤੇਰੋ ਮਾਨੁ॥ ਸਗਲ ਘਟਾ ਕਉ ਦੇਵਹੁ ਦਾਨੁ॥ ਕਰਨ ਕਰਾਵਨਹਾਰ ਸੁਆਮੀ॥ ਸਗਲ ਘਟਾ ਕੇ ਅੰਤਰਜਾਮੀ॥ ਅਪਨੀ ਗਤਿ ਮਿਤਿ ਜਾਨਹੁ ਆਪੇ॥ ਆਪਨ ਸੰਗਿ ਆਪਿ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਰਾਤੇ॥ ਤੁਮਰੀ ਉਸਤਤਿ ਤੁਮ ਤੇ ਹੋਇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਅਵਰੁ ਨ ਜਾਨਸਿ ਕੋਇ॥ 7॥

Nirdhun kuou dhun(u) Tayro Naaoun. Nithaavayn kuou Naaoun Tayra thaaoun. Nimaanay kuou Prubh tayro maan(u). Sugul ghutaan kuou dayvhou daan(u).  Kern Kravunhaar Soami. Sugul ghutaan kay untarjaami. Apni gut(i) mit(i) jaanhou Aapay.  Aapun sung(i) Aap(i) Prubh raatay. Toomri oostut(i) Toom tay hoyay. Nanak uver(u) na jaanus(i) koyay.  7.

Word Meaning:   Nithaavayn = shelterless.   Nimaanay = without any recognition.    Ghutaan = hearts; people.   Daan(u) = necessities of life.   Kern = the doer; the source of.   Kraavunhaar = making others do.   Untarjaami = know-all, omniscient.   Gut(i) = high spiritual state.  Mit(i) = capability.    Raatay = imbued; mingled with.   Oostut(i) = praise; magnificence.    7. 

MEANING:  Your Naam is the wealth for the poor, and is the shelter for the shelter-less, oh Waheguru.  You are the pride of a man with no recognition in society, oh Waheguru. And You are the provider for all living beings.  You are the source of everything. You cause others to do whatever You wish them to do, and You are the know-all (omniscient) of all the hearts, oh Lord.  Only You know Your high spiritual state (Your capabilities), and Your measurements. Being present in every heart, You are mingled with everybody (and with Yourself).  Only You know Your magnificence - nobody else does – oh, Nanak.   7.

ਸਰਬ ਧਰਮ ਮਹਿ ਸ੍ਰੇਸਟ ਧਰਮੁ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੋ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਿ ਨਿਰਮਲ ਕਰਮੁ॥ ਸਗਲ ਕ੍ਰਿਆ ਮਹਿ ਊਤਮ ਕਿਰਿਆ॥ ਸਾਧ ਸੰਗਿ ਦੁਰਮਤਿ ਮਲੁ ਹਿਰਿਆ॥ ਸਗਲ ਉਦਮ ਮਹਿ ਉਦਮੁ ਭਲਾ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕਾ ਨਾਮੁ ਜਪਹੁ ਜੀਅ ਸਦਾ॥ ਸਗਲ ਬਾਨੀ ਮਹਿ ਅੰਮ੍ਰਿਤ ਬਾਨੀ॥ ਹਰਿ ਕੋ ਜਸੁ ਸੁਨਿ ਰਸਨ ਬਖਾਨੀ॥ ਸਗਲ ਥਾਨ ਤੇ ਓਹੁ ਊਤਮ ਥਾਨੁ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਿਹ ਘਟਿ ਵਸੈ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮੁ॥ 8॥3॥

Serb dherm meh(i) srayshut Dherm(u).  Hur(i) ko Naam(u) jup(i) nirmul Kerm(u).  Sugul kirya meh(i) ootum kirya. Sadh sung(i) doormut(i) mul(u) hiriya.  Sugul oodum meh(i) Oodum bhulaa. Hur(i) ka Naam(u) jup-hou jea suda. Sugul bani meh(i) Amrit Bani. Hur(i) ko jus(u) soon(i) rusn bukhaani. Sugul thaan tay oh(u) ootum Thaan(u). Nanak jih ghut(i) vusaiy Hur(i) Naam(u). 

Word Meaning:  Dherm = human duty; faith.  Srayshut(i) = supreme.   Nirmul = pure; holy.   Kerm = deeds.    Kirya = acts.   Ootum = superior.   Doormut(i) = evil thinking.  Hiriya = remove; wash away.   Oodum = initiative; effort.   Bani = conversations; religious compositions.   Rusun = tongue.    Bukhaani = recite.   Thaan = place; heart.    8.

MEANING: Recitation of Waheguru’s Naam is the holiest deed, and is the best among all the faiths.  To wash away the filth of ill thinking (and of deeds) in the shelter of the Guru is the supreme of all the acts (deeds).   The best initiative of all is to recite Waheguru’s Naam all the time, oh my mind.  Listening to Waheguru’s praise, and to recite that with the tongue, is the Amrit - best among all the conversations.  The heart, which remembers Waheguru at all times, is the best of all places.   8.3.

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਨਿਰਗੁਨੀਆਰ ਇਆਨਿਆ ਸੋ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ ਸਦਾ ਸਮਾਲਿ॥ ਜਿਨਿ ਕੀਆ ਤਿਸੁ ਚੀਤਿ ਰਖੁ ਨਾਨਕ ਨਿਬਹੀ ਨਾਲਿ॥ 1॥

Sloak(u): Nirgooniyaar iyaaniya, so Prubh(u) suda smaal(i). Jin(i) keeya Tis(u) cheet(i) rukh(u), Nanak nib-hee naal(i). 

Word Meaning:  Nirgooniyaar = without any virtue.  Iyaaniya = ignorant.  Smaal(i) = remember.     Nib-hee = will be with you (support you) till the end. 

MEANING: Remember Waheguru all the time, oh ignorant and virtue-less man.  Keep your Creator in mind, and He will support you all the way, oh Nanak.   1. 

ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਰਮਈਆ ਕੇ ਗੁਨ ਚੇਤਿ ਪਰਾਨੀ॥ ਕਵਨ ਮੂਲ ਤੇ ਕਵਨ ਦ੍ਰਿਸਟਾਨੀ॥ ਜਿਨਿ ਤੂ ਸਾਜਿ ਸਵਾਰਿ ਸੀਗਾਰਿਆ॥ ਗਰਭ ਅਗਨਿ ਮਹਿ ਜਿਨਹਿ ਉਬਾਰਿਆ॥ ਬਾਰ ਬਿਵਸਥਾ ਤੁਝਹਿ ਪਿਆਰੈ ਦੂਧ॥ ਭਰਿ ਜੋਬਨ ਭੋਜਨ ਸੁਖ ਸੂਧ॥ ਬਿਰਧਿ ਭਇਆ ਊਪਰਿ ਸਾਕ ਸੈਨ॥ ਮੁਖਿ ਅਪਿਆਉ ਬੈਠ ਕਉ ਦੈਨ॥

(ਅੰਗ 267)॥     ਇਹੁ ਨਿਰਗੁਨੁ ਗੁਨੁ ਕਛੂ ਨ ਬੂਝੈ॥ ਬਖਸਿ ਲੇਹੁ ਤਉ ਨਾਨਕ ਸੀਝੈ॥  1॥

Ashtpudi: Rumuyiya kay goon chayt(i) praani. Kuvun mool tay kuvun drishtaani. Jin(i) tu saaj(i) suvaar(i) seegaariya. Gerbh ugun(i) meh(i) Jineh(i) oobaariya. Baar bivustha toojheh(i) piyaaraiy doodh. Bhur(i) jobun bhojun sookh soodh. Birdh bhuyya, oopur saak saiyn. Mookh(i) apiyayo baiytth kuou daiyn.

(PAGE 267):                  Ehou nirgoon, goon kuchhoo na boojhaiy. Bukhsh(i) layhou tuou Nanak seejhaiy.   1.

Word Meaning: Rumuyiya = pervading everywhere (Waheguru).    Chayt(i) = remember.   Praani = man.   Kuvun = which.     Mool = base; origin.   Drishtaani. = is seen; looks like.    Saaj(i) = create.   Suvaar(i) = polished.    Seegaariya = adorned; gave the final finish.   Gerbh Ugun(i) = the heat of the womb.  Bivustha = age.   Piyaaraiy = feeds.     Bhur(i) Jobun = prime of youth.   Soodh = understanding.   Birdh = old.   Saak Saiyn = relatives and friends.   Apiyayo = food.      Nirgoon = without any quality (virtue); ungrateful.      Seejhaiy = succeeds.   1.

MEANING:  Remember (recall) the virtues (kindness) of Waheguru, oh man. Remember what your origin was (sperm and egg), and what your final form looks like now!  Remember the Waheguru who created, polished, and adorned you to the final finish, and who protected you from the otherwise unbearable heat of your mother’s womb.  He arranges for milk in your childhood; provides you with the foods you relish, provides all comforts, and the ability to think (make decisions) in the prime of your youth.  When you get old, there are relatives and friends to look after you and feed you (when you cannot take care of yourself). But the ungrateful man does not acknowledge any of these favours by the Lord; he can succeed (find salvation) only if Waheguru forgives him, oh Nanak.   1.

ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਧਰ ਊਪਰਿ ਸੁਖਿ ਬਸਹਿ॥ ਸੁਤ ਭ੍ਰਾਤ ਮੀਤ ਬਨਿਤਾ ਸੰਗਿ ਹਸਹਿ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਪੀਵਹਿ ਸੀਤਲ ਜਲਾ॥ ਸੁਖਦਾਈ ਪਵਨ ਪਾਵਕੁ ਅਮੁਲਾ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਭੋਗਹਿ ਸਭਿ ਰਸਾ॥ ਸਗਲ ਸਮਗ੍ਰੀ ਸੰਗਿ ਸਾਥਿ ਬਸਾ॥ ਦੀਨੇ ਹਸਤ ਪਾਵ ਕਰਨ ਨੇਤ੍ਰ ਰਸਨਾ॥ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਤਿਆਗਿ ਅਵਰ ਸੰਗਿ ਰਚਨਾ॥ ਐਸੇ ਦੋਖ ਮੂੜ ਅੰਧ ਬਿਆਪੇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਕਾਢਿ ਲੇਹੁ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਆਪੇ॥ 2॥

Jih persaad(i) dhur oopur(i) sookh(i) buseh(i). Soot, bhraat, meet, bunita, sung(i) huseh(i). Jih persaad(i) peeveh(i) seetul jula. Sookhdaayee puvun, paavuk(u) amoola. Jih persaad(i) bhogeh(i) subh(i) rusa. Sugaul smuggri sung(i) saath(i) busa. Deenay hust, paav, kern, naytur, rusna.  Tiseh(i) tiyaag(i) uver sung(i) ruchna.  Aiysay

doakh moorr undh biyaapay.  Nanak kaadh(i) layhou Prubh aapay.   2. 

Word Meaning: Persaad(i) = kindness.   Dhur = earth.     Buseh(i) = prevails.   Soot = sons.    Bunita = wife.  Seetul = cool.   Paavuk(u) = fire.   Amoola = priceless.    Rusa = tastes.   Smuggri = needed materials.  Hust = hands.   Kern = ears.    Uver = the other (Maya).   Ruchna = get involved.   Doakh = vices.   Biyaapay = impact.     2.

MEANING: (The man does not remember the kind Waheguru) because of whose benevolence, he has a comfortable life on the earth, and enjoys happiness among his sons, brothers, friends, and his wife.  It is because of Waheguru’s kindness that the man enjoys cool water, comforting air, and priceless fire. It is because of Waheguru’s kindness that the man enjoys a variety of tastes and has all the necessities for living. Waheguru has given the man hands, feet, ears, eyes, and the tongue (to enjoy life), but, ignoring Waheguru completely, the ungrateful man gets engrossed in Maya.    (Ignoring Waheguru), Many vices overpower the ignorant and the foolish man.  Only Waheguru can pull him out of such immoralities, oh Nanak.    2. 

ਆਦਿ ਅੰਤਿ ਜੋ ਰਾਖਨਹਾਰੁ॥ ਤਿਸ ਸਿਉ ਪ੍ਰੀਤਿ ਨ ਕਰੈ ਗਵਾਰੁ॥ ਜਾ ਕੀ ਸੇਵਾ ਨਵ ਨਿਧਿ ਪਾਵੈ॥ ਤਾ ਸਿਉ ਮੂੜਾ ਮਨ ਨਹੀ ਲਾਵੈ॥ ਜੋ ਠਾਕੁਰੁ ਸਦ ਸਦਾ ਹਜੂਰੇ॥ ਤਾ ਕਉ ਅੰਧਾ ਜਾਨਤ ਦੂਰੇ॥ ਜਾ ਕੀ ਟਹਲ ਪਾਵੈ ਦਰਗਹ ਮਾਨੁ॥ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਬਿਸਾਰੈ ਮੁਗਧੁ ਅਜਾਨੁ॥ ਸਦਾ ਸਦਾ ਇਹੁ ਭੂਲਨਹਾਰੁ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਰਾਖਨਹਾਰੁ ਅਪਾਰੁ॥ 3॥

Aad(i) unt(i) jo raakhunhaar. Tis siyoun preet(i) na kuraiy guvaar(u).  Ja kee sayva nuv nidh(i) paavaiy. Ta siyoun moorra mun nuhee laavaiy. Jo Tthaakur(u) sud suda hujooray. Ta kuou undha jaanut dooray. Ja kee tehl paavaiy Durgeh maan(u).  Tiseh(i) bisaaaiy moogudh(u ajaan(u). Suda suda ehou bhoolunhaar(u). Nanak Raakhunhaar(u) apaar(u).    3.

Word Meaning:   Aad(i) = the beginning.      Gvaar(u) = foolish.   Nuv Nidh(i) = the nine mythical treasures.   Moorra = foolish.   Hujooray = present; around.   Tehl = service to.   Moogudh(u) = imprudent.      Ajaan(u) = ignorant.   Bhoolunhaar = fallible; likely to make mistakes.    3.

MEANING: The foolish man does not love Waheguru, who protects him from the beginning to the end.  The foolish man does not show any interest in Waheguru, serving whom, he can enjoy the comforts of the nine mythical treasures of the world.  The blind (ignorant) man does not realize that the Lord is always very close to him; he rather thinks of Him as being far, far away. The imprudent and the ignorant man forgets Waheguru, serving whom he earns respect in His Court.  The man makes mistakes all the time, while the limitless Waheguru forgives him, oh Nanak. 3. 

ਰਤਨੁ ਤਿਆਗਿ ਕਉਡੀ ਸੰਗਿ ਰਚੈ॥ ਸਾਚੁ ਛੋਡਿ ਝੂਠ ਸੰਗਿ ਮਚੈ॥ ਜੋ ਛਡਨਾ ਸੁ ਅਸਥਿਰੁ ਕਰਿ ਮਾਨੈ॥ ਜੋ ਹੋਵਨ ਸੋ ਦੂਰਿ ਪਰਾਨੈ॥ ਛੋਡਿ ਜਾਇ ਤਿਸ ਕਾ ਸ੍ਰਮੁ ਕਰੈ॥ ਸੰਗਿ ਸਹਾਈ ਤਿਸੁ ਪਰਹਰੈ॥ ਚੰਦਨ ਲੇਪ ਉਤਾਰੈ ਧੋਇ॥ ਗਰਧਬ ਪ੍ਰੀਤਿ ਭਸਮ ਸੰਗਿ ਹੋਇ॥ ਅੰਧ ਕੂਪ ਮਹਿ ਪਤਿਤ ਬਿਕਰਾਲ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਕਾਢਿ ਲੇਹੁ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਦਇਆਲ॥ 4॥

Ruttan(u) tiyaag(u), kaudi sung(i) ruchaiy. Saach(u) chhoad(i), jhootth sung(i) muchaiy. 

Jo chhuddna su usthir(u) kur(i) maanaiy.  Jo hovun so door(i) praanaiy. Chhoad(i) 

jaayay, tis ka sherm(u) kuraiy.  Sung(i) suhaahi, Tis(u) perhuraiy. Chundun layp ootaaraiy, dhoyay. Gerdhub preet(i) bhusum sung(i) hoyay. Undh koop meh(i) putit bikraal. Nanak kaadh(i) layhou Prubh duyaal.    4.

Word Meaning: Ruttan(u) = (precious like a) jewel (Waheguru’s Naam).  Kaudi = (a worthless object like) a cowrie shell (Maya).  Ruchaiy = gets involved; seeks comfort.   Muchaiy = heats up; gets excited.  Asthir = permanent; everlasting.    Praanaiy = recognizes; thinks.   Sherm(u) = work hard.   Purhuraiy = stays away from.   Chundun Layp = a coating of sandalwood pulp. Gerdhub = donkey.     Bhusum = dust; ash.   Undh Koop = a dark well (of Maya).  Putit = immoral.  Bikraal = frightening.      4.

MEANING: (The ignorant) man ignores Waheguru’s precious Naam and seeks comfort in worthless Maya (which is short-lived).  He ignores his eternal companion -Waheguru’s Naam - and gets excited about the short-lived Maya. The man treats Maya (whom he has to leave behind) as his eternal possession, and thinks of Waheguru (and death), which are always close to him, as being far, far away.  He works hard to earn wealth, which he has to leave behind, and stays away from Waheguru, which is always with him to guard him.  Like a donkey, which loves dust, and will wash away a coating of the sandalwood pulp (which gives off sweet fragrance, and keeps the body cool in hot weather), the ignorant man ignores Waheguru (sandalwood) and embraces dust (Maya).  The fate of the immoral man, who is stuck in the dark well of Maya, is frightening. Please, pull the ignorant out of this dark well, oh kind Waheguru, prays Nanak.   4.

ਕਰਤੂਤਿ ਪਸੂ ਕੀ ਮਾਨਸ ਜਾਤਿ॥ ਲੋਕ ਪਚਾਰਾ ਕਰੈ ਦਿਨੁ ਰਾਤਿ॥ ਬਾਹਰਿ ਭੇਖ ਅੰਤਰਿ ਮਲੁ ਮਾਇਆ॥ ਛਪਸਿ ਨਾਹਿ ਕਛੁ ਕਰੈ ਛਪਾਇਆ॥ ਬਾਹਰਿ ਗਿਆਨ ਧਿਆਨ ਇਸਨਾਨ॥ ਅੰਤਰਿ ਬਿਆਪੈ ਲੋਭੁ ਸੁਆਨੁ॥ ਅੰਤਰਿ ਅਗਨਿ ਬਾਹਰਿ ਤਨੁ ਸੁਆਹ॥ ਗਲਿ ਪਾਥਰ ਕੈਸੇ ਤਰੈ ਅਥਾਹ॥ ਜਾ ਕੈ ਅੰਤਰਿ ਬਸੈ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ ਆਪਿ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤੇ ਜਨ ਸਹਜਿ ਸਮਾਤਿ॥ 5॥

Kertoot(i) pusoo kee, maanus jaat(i). Loak puchaara kuraiy din(u) raat(i). Baahur(i) bhaykh untar(i) mul(u) Maya. Chhupus(i) naah(i) kuchh(u) kuraiy chhupaaya. Baahur(i) giyaan, dhiyaan, ishnaan.  Untar(i) biyaapaiy lobh(u) sooaan(u) . Untar(i) ugun(i), baahur(i) tun(u) sooaah. Gul(i) paathur kaiysay turaiy athaah.  Ja kaiy untar(i) busaiy Prubh aap(i).  Nanak tay jun sehj(i) smaat(i).     5.

Word Meaning: Kertoot(i) = deeds.    Pusoo = animal.    Maanus = human.    Loak Puchaara = (show-off) to impress people.     Bhaykh(i) = (saintly) garb.   Mul(u) = filth.   Giyaan = (preaches) knowledge.   Dhiyaan = meditates.   Sooaan(u) = dog.   Ugun(i) = fire (of desires); anger.   Paathur = rock.   Athaah = unfathomable (sea of Maya).    Sehj(i) = state of blissfulness.    Smaat(i) = gets absorbed; becomes oblivious.    5.

MEANING:  The man has only the looks of a human; his deeds are like those of an animal. All his deeds are simply a show-off to impress people.  He wears a religious garb (to fool people), but his mind is filthy with the desire for Maya. But he cannot hide the reality even if he tries hard.  Outwardly, he gives the impression of a religious person by preaching morals, by meditating for long times, and by taking pilgrimages; but the dog of greed is always biting him inside (prompting him).  He coats his body with ashes to give the impression that he has renounced Maya, but the fire of desires is alive in his mind.  With the rock (the load of his sins) around his neck, how can he swim across the unfathomable sea of Maya? Those individuals who have Waheguru 

enshrined in their mind become fully detached from Maya in the blissful state,  oh 

Nanak.  5.

ਸੁਨਿ ਅੰਧਾ ਕੈਸੇ ਮਾਰਗੁ ਪਾਵੈ॥ ਕਰੁ ਗਹਿ ਲੇਹੁ ਓੜਿ ਨਿਬਹਾਵੈ॥ ਕਹਾ ਬੁਝਾਰਤਿ ਬੂਝੈ ਡੋਰਾ॥ ਨਿਸਿ ਕਹੀਐ ਤਉ ਸਮਝੈ ਭੋਰਾ॥ ਕਹਾ ਬਿਸਨ ਪਦ ਗਾਵੈ ਗੁੰਗ॥ ਜਤਨ ਕਰੈ ਤਉ ਭੀ ਸੁਰ ਭੰਗ॥ ਕਹ ਪਿੰਗੁਲ ਪਰਬਤ ਪਰ ਭਵਨ॥ ਨਹੀ ਹੋਤ ਊਹਾ ਉਸੁ ਗਵਨ॥ ਕਰਤਾਰ ਕਰੁਣਾਮੈ ਦੀਨੁ ਬੇਨਤੀ ਕਰੈ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤੁਮਰੀ ਕਿਰਪਾ ਤਰੈ॥ 6॥

Soon(i) undha kaiysay maarug(u) paavaiy. Ker(u) geh(i) layhou, oarr(i) nibhaavaiy. Kuhaa boojhaarut(i) boojhaiy dora. Nis(i) kuhiyaiy, tuou sumjhaiy bhora. Kuhaa Bshan pud gaavaiy goong. Jutun kuraiy, tuou bhee soor bhung.  Keh pingul perbut per bhuvun. Nuhee hoat, oohaan oos(u guvun. Kartar, kroona-maiy, deen(u) baynti kuraiy. Nanak Toomri kirpa turaiy.   6.

Word Meaning: Ker(u) = hand.   Geh(i) = hold.   Oarr(i) Nibhaavaiy = fulfil obligation; can reach destination.  Boojhaarut(i) = riddle.   Dora = deaf.   Nis(i) = night.   Bhora = morning; day.  Bishun Pud = devotional song.   Goong = mute.   Soor = voice.  Bhung = breaks down.   Pingul = lame.  Bhuvun = travel.    Guvun = go; reach.   Kroonamaiy = source of compassion.   Deen(u) = helpless.   Turaiy = find salvation.     6.

MEANING: A blind person cannot follow their path just by listening (to the instructions). But he will reach his destination if held by the hand.  A deaf person cannot understand a riddle; if he is told it is nighttime, he thinks it is daytime.  A mute cannot sing a devotional song (or another song for that matter); his voice will break down (split) even if he tries very hard.   A lame person cannot walk to climb a mountain because they cannot even get there.  (Similarly), How can a man, who is spiritually blind, deaf, lame and mute in the love for Maya, swim across the deep sea of Maya?)  The humble Nanak prays that if the source of compassion, Waheguru, is kind, the helpless man can swim across the world sea (find salvation).   6.

ਸੰਗਿ ਸਹਾਈ ਸੁ ਆਵੈ ਨ ਚੀਤਿ॥ ਜੋ ਬੈਰਾਈ ਤਾ ਸਿਉ ਪ੍ਰੀਤਿ॥ ਬਲੂਆ ਕੈ ਗ੍ਰਿਹ ਭੀਤਰਿ ਬਸੈ॥ ਅਨਦ ਕੇਲ ਮਾਇਆ ਰੰਗਿ ਰਸੈ॥ ਦ੍ਰਿੜੁ ਕਰਿ ਮਾਨੈ ਮਨਹਿ ਪ੍ਰਤੀਤਿ॥ ਕਾਲੁ ਨ ਆਵੈ ਮੂੜੇ ਚੀਤਿ॥ ਬੈਰ ਬਿਰੋਧ ਕਾਮ ਕ੍ਰੋਧ ਮੋਹ॥ ਝੂਠ ਬਿਕਾਰ ਮਹਾ ਲੋਭ ਧ੍ਰੋਹ॥

(ਅੰਗ 268)॥  ਇਆਹੂ ਜੁਗਤਿ ਬਿਹਾਨੇ ਕਈ ਜਨਮ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਰਾਖਿ ਲੇਹੁ ਆਪਨ ਕਰਿ ਕਰਮ॥7

Sung(i) suhaayee, su aavaiy na cheet(i). Jo bairaayee, ta siyoun preet(i). Bulooa kaiy greh(i) bheetur(i) busaiy. Anund kayl Maya rung(i) rusaiy.  Drirr(u) kur(i) maanaiy muneh(i) purteet(i). Kaal(u) na aavaiy moorraiy cheet(i). Baiyr, birodh, kaam, krodh, moh. Jhootth bikaar, muha, loabh, dhroh. 

(PAGE 268):    Ihaaoo joogut(i) bihaanay kuyee junum. Nanak, raakh(i) layhou aapun kur(i) kerm.  7.

Word Meaning: Suhaayee = helper; supporter.  Cheet(i) = in mind.   Bairaayee = enemy; vices     Bulooa = sand.    Greh(i) = house.   Anund Kayl = entertainment.   Rusaiy = enjoys the tastes.     Drirr(u) = full faith.  Perteet(i) = trust; dependence.   Kaal(u) = death.   Baiyr = animosity.     Birodh = opposition.    Dhroh = deceit.   Joogut(i) = lifestyle.   Bihaanay = spent.   Kerm = benevolence.    7.

MEANING:  The man does not remember Waheguru, who is always with him to support him, but loves Maya, who is really his enemy (because it gives birth to vices and to rebirths).  The support of Maya is just like a house of sand (which can collapse at any time) in which the man is enjoying the temporary bliss and comforts of different tastes of Maya.  He has full faith in his everlasting dependence on Maya and never thinks of death. He spends many, many lives based on enmity, animosity, lust, rage, attachment, lies, immorality, greed, and big deceit.  I, Nanak, pray to Waheguru to protect (salvage) such an immoral man.   7.

ਤੂ ਠਾਕੁਰੁ ਤੁਮ ਪਹਿ ਅਰਦਾਸਿ॥ ਜੀਉ ਪਿੰਡੁ ਸਭੁ ਤੇਰੀ ਰਾਸਿ॥ ਤੁਮ ਮਾਤ ਪਿਤਾ ਹਮ ਬਾਰਿਕ ਤੇਰੇ॥ ਤੁਮਰੀ ਕ੍ਰਿਪਾ ਮਹਿ ਸੂਖ ਘਨੇਰੇ॥ ਕੋਇ ਨ ਜਾਨੈ ਤੁਮਰਾ ਅੰਤੁ॥ ਊਚੇ ਤੇ ਊਚਾ ਭਗਵੰਤ॥ ਸਗਲ ਸਮਗ੍ਰੀ ਤੁਮਰੈ ਸੂਤ੍ਰਿ ਧਾਰੀ॥ ਤੁਮ ਤੇ ਹੋਇ ਸੁ ਆਗਿਆਕਾਰੀ॥ ਤੁਮਰੀ ਗਤਿ ਮਿਤਿ ਤੁਮ ਹੀ ਜਾਨੀ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਦਾਸ ਸਦਾ ਕੁਰਬਾਨੀ॥ 8॥4॥

Tu Tthaakur(u) Toom peh(i) ardaas(i). Jeao, pind(u) subh(u) Tayri raas(i).  Toom, maat, pita, hum baarik Tayray. Toomri kirpa meh(i) sookh ghunayray. Koyay na jaanaiy Toomra unt(u).  Oochay tay oocha Bhugvunt.  Sugul smuggri Toomraiy sootur(i) dhaari.  Toom tay hoyay su aagiyakaari.  Toomri gut(i) mit(i) Toom hee jaani. Nanak daas suda koorbaani.  8.4. 

Word Meaning: Tthaakur(u) = Lord.   Ardaas(i) = prayer.   Raas(i) = investment; ownership.    Ghunayray = immense.     Smuggri = all ingredients; everything.  Sootur(i) = Huqam; laws.  Dhaari = supported; bound by.   Aagiyakaari = obedient.   Gut(i) = high spiritual state, grandeur.    Mit(i) = measurements.     8. 

MEANING:  You are the Lord, and I pray to You that we owe our life and body to You, oh Waheguru.   You are our mother, and our father, and we are Your children, oh Waheguru.  Your shelter is a source of immense comfort for us. Nobody has ever found Your limits because You are higher and higher.  All bodies are bound by Your laws, and whatever You have created, obeys You (Your Huqam).  Only You know Your grandeur, and Your measurements. I, Nanak, am Your slave, and always willing to sacrifice my life for You, oh Waheguru.   8.4.

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਦੇਨਹਾਰੁ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਛੋਡਿ ਕੈ ਲਾਗਹਿ ਆਨ ਸੁਆਇ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਕਹੂ ਨ ਸੀਝਈ ਬਿਨੁ ਨਾਵੈ ਪਤਿ ਜਾਇ॥ 1॥

Sloak(u): Daynhaar(u) Prubh chhoadkaiy, laageh(i) aan sooaayay. Nanak kuhoo na seejhuyee, bin(u) Naavaiy put(i) jaayay.   1.

Word Meaning: Daynhaar(u) = the provider.   Aan sooaayay = other tastes.   Seejhuyee = succeeds.   Put(i) = honour; dignity.   Jaayay = goes away; loses. 

MEANING: Ignoring the provider Waheguru, the man is enjoying the different tastes of Maya. (Because of such tastes), he will never succeed (achieve his real objective – unity with God), and is losing his dignity (is being robbed of his virtues by the five demons) without Waheguru’s Naam, oh Nanak.   1.

ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਦਸ ਬਸਤੂ ਲੈ ਪਾਛੈ ਪਾਵੈ॥ ਏਕ ਬਸਤੁ ਕਾਰਨਿ ਬਿਖੋਟਿ ਗਵਾਵੈ॥ ਏਕ ਭੀ ਨ ਦੇਇ ਦਸ ਭੀ ਹਿਰਿ ਲੇਇ॥ ਤਉ ਮੂੜਾ ਕਹੁ ਕਹਾ ਕਰੇਇ॥ ਜਿਸੁ ਠਾਕੁਰ ਸਿਉ ਨਾਹੀ ਚਾਰਾ॥ ਤਾ ਕਉ ਕੀਜੈ ਸਦ ਨਮਸਕਾਰਾ॥ ਜਾ ਕੈ ਮਨਿ ਲਾਗਾ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ ਮੀਠਾ॥ ਸਰਬ ਸੂਖ ਤਾਹੂ ਮਨਿ ਵੂਠਾ॥ ਜਿਸੁ ਜਨ ਅਪਨਾ ਹੁਕਮੁ ਮਨਾਇਆ॥ ਸਰਬ ਥੋਕ ਨਾਨਕ ਤਿਨਿ ਪਾਇਆ॥ 1॥

Ashtpudi: Dus bustoo laiy paachhaiy paavaiy. Aik bust(u) kaarun(i) bikhoat(i) guvaavaiy. Aik bhee na dayay, dus bhee hir(i) layay. Tuou moorra kuhou kuhaa kurayay. Jis(u) Tthaakur siyoun naahi chaara. Ta kuou keejaiy sud numskaara. Ja kaiy mun(i) laaga Prubh(u) meettha. Serb sookh taahoo mun(i) voottha.  Jis(u) jun Apna Huqam(u) munaaya. Serb thoak Nanak tin(i) paaya.  1.

Word Meaning: Paachhaiy Paavaiy = carries on the back; takes possession.  Bikhoat(i) = purity; trust.    Hir(i) = steal; take back.   Moorra = foolish.   Chaara = cannot compete against; are helpless before Him.   Voottha = dwells; are acquired.   Thoak = products.   1.

MEANING: Provided by Waheguru, the man takes possession of ten (many) items (of his need), but if he is unable to get only one more, (he protests against that), as a result of which he loses his trust (because of his greed) just for one item.  What will the foolish do if Waheguru does not give him the desired one item and takes back the ten He had already given him?  One should always bow to the Lord (be thankful to Him) before whom one is helpless.  An individual who finds Waheguru sweet in his mind acquires all the comforts in his mind. An individual who obeys Waheguru's Huqam acquires everything of his choice, oh Nanak.     1.

ਅਗਨਤ ਸਾਹੁ ਅਪਨੀ ਦੇ ਰਾਸਿ॥ ਖਾਤ ਪੀਤ ਬਰਤੈ ਅਨਦ ਉਲਾਸਿ॥ ਅਪੁਨੀ ਅਮਾਨ ਕਛੁ ਬਹੁਰਿ ਸਾਹੁ ਲੇਇ॥ ਅਗਿਆਨੀ ਮਨਿ ਰੋਸੁ ਕਰੇਇ॥ ਅਪਨੀ ਪਰਤੀਤਿ ਆਪ ਹੀ ਖੋਵੈ॥ਬਹੁਰਿ ਉਸ ਕਾ ਬਿਸਵਾਸੁ ਨ ਹੋਵੈ॥ ਜਿਸ ਕੀ ਬਸਤੁ ਤਿਸੁ ਆਗੈ ਰਾਖੈ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕੀ ਆਗਿਆ ਮਾਨੈ ਮਾਥੈ॥ ਉਸ ਤੇ ਚਉਗੁਨ ਕਰੈ ਨਿਹਾਲੁ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਸਾਹਿਬੁ ਸਦਾ ਦਇਆਲੁ॥ 2॥

Ugunut Shah(u) apni day raas(i).  Khaat peet bertaiy anund oolaas(i). Apuni amaan kuchh(u) buhour(i) Shah(u) layay. Ugiyaani mun(i) roas(u) kurayay. Apni perteet(i) aap hee khovaiy.  Buhour(i) oos ka bisvaas(u) na hovaiy. Jis kee bust(u) tis(u) aagaiy raakhaiy. Prubh kee aagiya maanaiy maathaiy. Oos tay chaugun kuraiy nihaal(u).  Nanak Sahib(u) suda duyaal(u).  2.

Word Meaning:  Ugunt = beyond measurements.     Shah(u) = money Lord; king; provider of everything.  Raas(i) = capital; the articles needed for life.   Anund Oolaas(i) = happiness and gusto (enthusiasm).    Amaan = placed in trust.   Buhour(i) = (takes) back. Roas(i) = complains; protests.   Perteet(i) = trust; impression.  Bisvaas(u) = trust.  Chaugun = four times.   Nihaal(u) = blesses.     2.

MEANING:  The King-Waheguru provides the needed articles beyond measurements to the living beings, who eat, drink, and use them with gusto to their satisfaction.  If Waheguru takes back some of the articles given (which are like a trust fund), the ignorant man protests against it in his mind.  Thus, he loses his own trust and is no longer trusted by Waheguru.  If he obeys Waheguru’s Will, and happily offers some of Waheguru’s gifts back to Him (gives to the needy or the meaningful charities), Waheguru blesses him four (many) times more, as He is always kind, oh Nanak.  2.

ਅਨਿਕ ਭਾਤਿ ਮਾਇਆ ਕੇ ਹੇਤ॥ ਸਰਪਰ ਹੋਵਤ ਜਾਨੁ ਅਨੇਤ॥ ਬਿਰਖ ਕੀ ਛਾਇਆ ਸਿਉ ਰੰਗੁ ਲਾਵੈ॥ ਓਹ ਬਿਨਸੈ ਉਹੁ ਮਨਿ ਪਛੁਤਾਵੈ॥ਜੋ ਦੀਸੈ ਸੋ ਚਾਲਨਹਾਰੁ॥ ਲਪਟਿ ਰਹਿਓ ਤਹ ਅੰਧ ਅੰਧਾਰੁ॥ ਬਟਾਊ ਸਿਉ ਜੋ ਲਾਵੈ ਨੇਹ॥ ਤਾ ਕਉ ਹਾਥਿ ਨ ਆਵੈ ਕੇਹ॥ ਮਨ ਹਰਿ ਕੇ ਨਾਮ ਕੀ ਪ੍ਰੀਤਿ ਸੁਖਦਾਈ॥ ਕਰਿ ਕਿਰਪਾ ਨਾਨਕ ਆਪਿ ਲਏ ਲਾਈ॥ 3॥

Anik bhaant(i) Maya kay hayt. Surpur hovut jaan(u) anayt. Birkh kee chhaaya siyoun rung(u) laavaiy. Oh binsaiy, ohou mun(i) puchhutaavaiy.  Jo deesaiy so chaalunhaar(u). Lupt(i) ruhiyo teh undh undhaar(u).  Butaaoo siyoun jo laavaiy nayh. Ta kay haath(i) na aavaiy kayh. Mun Hur(i) kay Naam kee preet(i) sookhdaayee. Kur(i) kirpa Nanak aap(i) luyay laayee.   3.

Word Meaning:   Hayt = for; love.   Surpur = for sure.   Anayt = perishable.   Rung(u) = love.  Binsaiy = vanishes.   Chaalunhaar = perishable.   Undhaar(u) = darkness (ignorant).   Butaaoo = visitor.    Nayh = love.  Sookhdaayee = comforting.     3.

MEANING: The man loves (enjoys) countless different forms of Maya, but he must realize for sure that they are not going to last forever.  They are like the shade of a tree which vanishes (changes) with the sun, and the man who depends on it has to repent.  Every visible thing is perishable, and the ignorant and the blind man is clinging to them. A person who falls in love with a visitor accomplishes nothing.   Only the love for Waheguru’s Naam is comforting (source of bliss), but only that individual receives it who Waheguru Himself gives it kindly, oh Nanak.    3.

ਮਿਥਿਆ ਤਨੁ ਧਨੁ ਕੁਟੰਬੁ ਸਬਾਇਆ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਹਉਮੈ ਮਮਤਾ ਮਾਇਆ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਰਾਜ ਜੋਬਨ ਧਨ ਮਾਲ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਕਾਮ ਕ੍ਰੋਧ ਬਿਕਰਾਲ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਰਥ ਹਸਤੀ ਅਸਵ ਬਸਤ੍ਰਾ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਰੰਗਿ ਸੰਗਿ ਮਾਇਆ ਪੇਖਿ ਹਸਤਾ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਧ੍ਰੋਹ ਮੋਹ ਅਭਿਮਾਨੁ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਆਪਸ ਉਪਰਿ ਕਰਤ ਗੁਮਾਨੁ॥ ਅਸਥਿਰੁ ਭਗਤਿ ਸਾਧ ਕੀ ਸਰਨ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਜਪਿ ਜਪਿ ਜੀਵੈ ਹਰਿ ਕੇ ਚਰਨ॥4॥

Mithiya tun(u) dhun(u) kootumb(u) subaaya. Mithiya haumein mumta Maya.  Mithiya raj jobun dhun maal. Mithiya kaam krodh bikraal. Mithiya ruth hustee, ashuv bustra. Mithiya rung(i) sung(i) Maya paykh(i) husta. Mithiya dhroh, moh, abhimaan(u).  Mithiya aapus oopur(i) kert goomaan(u).  Usthir(u) bhugut(i) Sadh kee shern. Nanak jup(i) jup(i) jeevaiy Hur(i) kay Churn.  4.

Word Meaning: Mithiya = false; short-lived.  Kootumb(u) = family.   Mumta = worldly attachments.    Jobun = youthfulness.    Bikraal = frightening.   Ruth = chariots.   Hustee = elephants.   Ashuv = horses.    Paykh(i) = on seeing; enjoying.   Dhroh = deceit; guile; fraud.   Asthir(u) = permanent; eternal.     4.

MEANING: Man’s body, his wealth, and his family are all false (short-lived), and so are one’s ego, selfishness, and his attachment to Maya.  The estate, youthfulness, wealth, and other belongings are equally false, and so are lust and frightening rage.  None of the chariots, elephants, horses, and expensive clothes will last forever; and short-lived are the enjoyments of Maya, enjoying which, the man feels happy.  Short-lived are deceitfulness, worldly attachments, conceit, and one’s self-pride. Only the worship of Waheguru in the shelter of the Guru is everlasting.  I survive by worshipping Waheguru’s Feet (His Naam), oh Nanak.   4.

ਮਿਥਿਆ ਸ੍ਰਵਨ ਪਰ ਨਿੰਦਾ ਸੁਨਹਿ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਹਸਤ ਪਰ ਦਰਬ ਕਉ ਹਿਰਹਿ॥

(ਅੰਗ 269)॥                  ਮਿਥਿਆ ਨੇਤ ੍ਰਪੇਖਤ ਪਰ ਤ੍ਰਿਅ ਰੂਪਾਦਿ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਰਸਨਾ ਭੋਜਨ ਅਨ ਸਵਾਦ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਚਰਨ ਪਰ ਬਿਕਾਰ ਕਉ ਧਾਵਹਿ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਮਨ ਪਰ ਲੋਭ ਲੁਭਾਵਹਿ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਤਨ ਨਹੀ ਪਰਉਪਕਾਰਾ॥ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਬਾਸੁ ਲੇਤ ਬਿਕਾਰਾ॥ ਬਿਨੁ ਬੂਝੇ ਮਿਥਿਆ ਸਭ ਭਏ॥ ਸਫਲ ਦੇਹ ਨਾਨਕ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਮ ਲਏ॥ 5॥

Mithiya servun per ninda sooneh(i).  Mithiya hust per derb kuou hireh(i). 

(PAGE 269):                            Mithiya naytur paykhut per tirya roopaad(i).  Mithiya rusna bhojun un suvaad. Mithiya churn, per bikaar kuou dhaaveh(i). Mithiya mun per loabh loobhaaveh(i). Mithiya tun, nuhee peroopkaara.  Mithiya baas(u) layt bikaara.  Bin(u) boojhay mithiya subh bhuyay. Suful dayh, Nanak Hur(i) Hur(i) Naam luyyay.  5.

Word Meaning: Mithiya = false; unsuccessful; condemnable.  Servun = ears.   Per = of others.   Ninda = malicious criticism; vilification.    Hust = hands.    Derb = wealth.   Hireh(i) = steal.   Triya = women.   Roopaad(i) = beauty and the like.   Un = others.   Bikaar = evils.   Dhaaveh(i) = rush to.   Loobhaaveh(i) = is attracted; is captivated.   Peroopkaara = to help the needy.   Baas(u) = nose; smell.   Bin Boojhay = without realization.   Suful = successful.   Dayh = body. 

MEANING: The ears, which enjoy listening to the vilification of others, are false (filthy, and not going to live forever), nor are the hands going to live forever that steal the wealth of others.  Reprehensible are the eyes that enjoy the beauty, etc., of other women, and so is the tongue that enjoys the tastes of other foods (than Waheguru’s Naam). Condemnable (unsuccessful) are the feet which rush to commit evil against others, and condemnable is the mind that is captivated by the greed for others’ belongings.  Condemnable is the human body, which does not do any favour to the needy, and so is the nose that smells vices (vice-provoking fragrances).   All parts of the body are false (filthy) without the realization of (Waheguru’s Naam); only that body is successful (in achieving union with Waheguru) which recites Waheguru’s Naam, oh Nanak.   5.

ਬਿਰਥੀ ਸਾਕਤ ਕੀ ਆਰਜਾ॥ ਸਾਚ ਬਿਨਾ ਕਹ ਹੋਵਤ ਸੂਚਾ॥ ਬਿਰਥਾ ਨਾਮ ਬਿਨਾ ਤਨੁ ਅੰਧ॥ ਮੁਖਿ ਆਵਤ ਤਾ ਕੈ ਦੁਰਗੰਧ॥ ਬਿਨੁ ਸਿਮਰਨ ਦਿਨੁ ਰੈਨਿ ਬ੍ਰਿਥਾ ਬਿਹਾਇ॥ ਮੇਘ ਬਿਨਾ ਜਿਉ ਖੇਤੀ ਜਾਇ॥ ਗੋਬਿਦ ਭਜਨ ਬਿਨੁ ਬ੍ਰਿਥੇ ਸਭ ਕਾਮ॥ ਜਿਉ ਕਿਰਪਨ ਕੇ ਨਿਰਾਰਥ ਦਾਮ॥ ਧੰਨਿ ਧੰਨਿ ਤੇ ਜਨ ਜਿਹ ਘਟਿ ਬਸਿਓ ਹਰਿ ਨਾਉ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤਾ ਕੇ ਬਲਿ ਬਲਿ ਜਾਉ॥ 6॥

Birthee saakut kee aarja. Saach bina keh hovut soocha.  Birtha Naam bina tun(u) undh.  Mookh(i) aavut ta kaiy doorgundh.  Bin(u) simrun din raiyn(i) birtha bihaayay. Maygh bina jiyoun khayti jaayay. Gobind bhujun bin(u) birthay subh kaam. Jiyoun kirpun kay niraaruth daam. Dhunn(i) dhunn(i) tay jun jih ghut(i) busiyo Hur(i) Naaoun. Nanak ta kay bul(i) bul(i) jaaoun.  6. 

Word Meaning:  Birthi = empty; worthless.   Saakut = money-lover; disconnected from God.   Aarja = life.  Soocha = pure.   Undh = blind; dark.    Doorgundh = foul smell (of malice).   Bihaayay = is spent.   Maygh = clouds (rain).   Khayti = crop.   Kirpun = money-lover.      Niraaruth = without any meaning; useless.   Daam = money.   Dhunn(i) = praise-worthy; lucky.    Bul(i) = sacrifice life.     6.

MEANING: Worthless is the life of the man who is disconnected from Waheguru (even if he performs countless rituals to cleanse the body) because the human mind cannot be purified without Waheguru’s Naam. Without Waheguru’s Naam, the human body is dark (ignorant) and useless.  The mouth (the tongue which does not recite Waheguru’s Naam) smells foul (because of malicious criticism of others).  Just like a crop dies without rain, the day and night of the man pass by uselessly without Waheguru’s Naam.  Just like the money of a money-lover goes to waste (because he does not spend it), all other deeds of a man go to waste (are useless) without the worship of Waheguru. Those individuals are praiseworthy who have Waheguru’s Naam enshrined in their hearts.  I will gladly sacrifice my life for them, oh Nanak.   6.

ਰਹਤ ਅਵਰ ਕਛੁ ਅਵਰ ਕਮਾਵਤ॥ ਮਨਿ ਨਹੀ ਪ੍ਰੀਤਿ ਮੁਖਹੁ ਗੰਢ ਲਾਵਤ॥ ਜਾਨਨਹਾਰ ਪ੍ਰਭੂ ਪਰਬੀਨ॥ ਬਾਹਰਿ ਭੇਖ ਨ ਕਾਹੂ ਭੀਨ॥ ਅਵਰ ਉਪਦੇਸੈ ਆਪਿ ਨ ਕਰੈ॥ ਆਵਤ ਜਾਵਤ ਜਨਮੈ

ਮਰੈ॥ ਜਿਸ ਕੈ ਅੰਤਰਿ ਬਸੈ ਨਿਰੰਕਾਰੁ॥ ਤਿਸ ਕੀ ਸੀਖ ਤਰੈ ਸੰਸਾਰੁ॥ ਜੋ ਤੁਮ ਭਾਨੇ ਤਿਨ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ

ਜਾਤਾ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਉਨ ਜਨ ਚਰਨ ਪਰਾਤਾ॥ 7॥

Rehut uver, kuchh(u) uver kmaavut. Mun(i) nuhee preet(i) mookh-houn gundh laavut. Jaanunhaar Prubhoo perbeen.  Baahur(i) bhaykh na kaahoo bheen.  Uver oopdayshaiy, aap(i) na kuraiy. Aavut, jaavut, junmaiy muraiy.  Jis kaiy untar(i) busaiy Nirunkaar(u). Tis kee seekh turaiy sunsaar(u). Jo Toom bhaanay, tin Prubh(u) jaata. Nanak, oon jun churn praata.  7.

Word Meaning:  Rehut = physical outlook; lifestyle.    Uver = other; different.    Kmaavut = performance (of deeds).   Gundh = establish connection.   Jaanunhaar = omniscient.  Perbeen = very wise.   Bhaykh = garb; attire.   Bheen = moved; is pleased.   Oopdayshaiy = preaches.    Nirunkaar(u) = formless Waheguru.  Seekh = advice.   Bhaanay = liked.    Praata = touches.     7.

MEANING:  Man’s outlook (outward lifestyle) often belies his deeds.  He has no love for Waheguru, but tries to connect with Him through words alone. However, the omniscient Waheguru is perfectly wise and is not moved by a person's outward appearance.  The man preaches to others, but does not follow his own advice.  Because of his ignoble deeds, he comes and goes, takes birth and dies.  The world finds salvation by the advice of the devotee who has Waheguru’s Naam embedded in his heart.  But only those devotees make a connection with You (enshrine Your Naam in their heart) whom You like, oh Waheguru.  I wish to touch the feet of such devotees, oh Nanak.    7.   

ਕਰਉ ਬੇਨਤੀ ਪਾਰਬ੍ਰਹਮੁ ਸਭੁ ਜਾਨੈ॥ ਅਪਨਾ ਕੀਆ ਆਪਹਿ ਮਾਨੈ॥ ਆਪਹਿ ਆਪ ਆਪਿ ਕਰਤ ਨਿਬੇਰਾ॥ ਕਿਸੈ ਦੂਰਿ ਜਨਾਵਤ ਕਿਸੈ ਬੁਝਾਵਤ ਨੇਰਾ॥ ਉਪਾਵ ਸਿਆਨਪ ਸਗਲ ਤੇ ਰਹਤ॥ ਸਭੁ ਕਛੁ ਜਾਨੈ ਆਤਮ ਕੀ ਰਹਤ॥ ਜਿਸੁ ਭਾਵੈ ਤਿਸੁ ਲਏ ਲੜਿ ਲਾਇ॥ ਥਾਨ ਥਨੰਤਰਿ ਰਹਿਆ ਸਮਾਇ॥ ਸੋ ਸੇਵਕੁ ਜਿਸੁ ਕਿਰਪਾ ਕਰੀ॥ ਨਿਮਖ ਨਿਮਖ ਜਪਿ ਨਾਨਕ ਹਰੀ॥ 8॥5॥

Kuroun baynti, Parbraham subh(u) jaanaiy. Apna keeya, Aapeh(i) maanaiy. Aapeh(i) Aap, Aap(i) kert nibayra. Kisaiy door(i) junaavut, kisaiy boojhaavut nayra. Oopaav siyaanup sugul tay rehut. Subh(u) kuchh(u) jaanaiy aatum kee rehut.  Jis bhaavaiy, tis(u) luyay lurr(i) laayay. Thaan thununtar(i) ruhiya smaayay.  So sayvuk(u), jis(u) kirpa kuree. Nimukh, nimukh jup(i) Nanak Huree.   8.5.

Word Meaning: Jaanaiy = knows.   Maanaiy = accepts; approves.   Nibayra = decision.   Oopaav = techniques.  Siyaanup = wisdom.   Rehut = unaffected; does not recognize.   Rehut = have in mind.    Lurr(i) Laayay = connects with Him.   Thaan Thununtar(i) = everywhere.     Smaayay = present.   Nimukh = moment.    8.

MEANING: Waheguru already knows the request I am about to make.  Being present in every heart, He makes people do things He likes and approves of.  He alone makes His own decisions; to some, He gives the impression that He is far away, while to some others, He enlightens them that He is very close. Waheguru is unaffected by the techniques people use to impress Him, or by their wisdom. He knows everything that people have on their minds.   Waheguru is present everywhere, and He connects the individuals with Him whom He likes.  Only that individual serves Him whom He is kind to.  Recite His Naam every moment, oh Nanak.    8.5.

ਸਲੋਕੁ॥ ਕਾਮ ਕ੍ਰੋਧ ਅਰੁ ਲੋਭ ਮੋਹ ਬਿਨਸਿ ਜਾਇ ਅਹੰਮੇਵ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਸਰਣਾਗਤੀ ਕਰਿ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦੁ

ਗੁਰਦੇਵ॥ 1॥

Sloak(u): Kaam, kroadh, ur(u) lobh, moh, binus(i) jaayay uhunmayv.  Nanak Prubh shernagti, kur(i) persaad(u) Gurdev.  1.

Word Meaing:  Kaam = lust.    Binus(i) = vanish.   Uhunmayv = ego.   Shernaagti = (come to seek) shelter.     Persaad(u) = kindness.   Gurdev = the great Guru. 

MEANING:  All lust, rage, greed, worldly attachments, and one’s ego vanish in Waheguru’s shelter. I, Nanak, too have come to You shelter, please be kind to me.  1.

ਅਸਟਪਦੀ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਛਤੀਹ ਅੰਮ੍ਰਿਤ ਖਾਹਿ॥ ਤਿਸੁ ਠਾਕੁਰ ਕਉ ਰਖੁ ਮਨ ਮਾਹਿ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਸੁਗੰਧਤ ਤਨਿ ਲਾਵਹਿ॥ ਤਿਸ ਕਉ ਸਿਮਰਤ ਪਰਮ ਗਤਿ ਪਾਵਹਿ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਬਸਹਿ ਸੁਖ ਮੰਦਰਿ॥ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਧਿਆਇ ਸਦਾ ਮਨ ਅੰਦਰਿ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਗ੍ਰਿਹ ਸੰਗਿ ਸੁਖ ਬਸਨਾ॥ ਆਠ ਪਹਰ ਸਿਮਰਹੁ ਤਿਸੁ ਰਸਨਾ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਰੰਗ ਰਸ ਭੋਗ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਸਦਾ ਧਿਆਈਐ ਧਿਆਵਨ ਜੋਗ॥ 1॥

Ashtpudi: Jih persaad(i) chhutti amrit khaah(i).  Tis Tthaakur kuou rukh(u) mun maah(i).  Jih persaad(i) soogundhut tun(i) laaveh(i). Tis kuou simrut Perm gut(i) paaveh(i). Jih persaad(i) buseh(i) sookh mundar(i). Tiseh(i) dhiyaayay suda mun undar(i).  Jih persaad(i) greh(i) sung(i) sookh busna.  Aatth pehr simerhou Tis(u) rusna. Jih persaad(i) rung rus bhoag. Nanak suda dhiyaayiyaiy, dhiyaavun joag.   1.

Word Meaning: Chhutti Amrit:  36 delicious foods (according to Indian culinary).   Tthaakur = Lord.   Soogundhut = fragrances.   Perm Gut(i) = highest spiritual state (union with God).     Mundar(i) = in the mansion.      Greh(i) = household.    Rung Rus Bhoag = fun activities; and tastes.      Dhiyaavun Joag = worthy of worship.    1.

MEANING: Keep the Lord, with whose grace you enjoy the 36 different kinds of delicious foods, always in your mind, oh my friend.   You attain the highest spiritual state (union with God) by reciting the Naam of the Lord, by whose grace you apply fragrances to your body. Recite the Naam of the Waheguru in your mind by whose grace you live comfortably in big mansions. Recite the Naam of the Waheguru twenty-four hours a day with your tongue, by whose grace you live comfortably with your family. Always worship Waheguru, who is worthy of worship, and by whose grace you enjoy the fun activities and different tastes of life, oh Nanak.    1. 

ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਪਾਟ ਪਟੰਬਰ ਹਢਾਵਹਿ॥ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਤਿਆਗਿ ਕਤ ਅਵਰ ਲੁਭਾਵਹਿ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਸੁਖਿ ਸੇਜ ਸੋਈਜੈ॥ ਮਨ ਆਠ ਪਹਰ ਤਾ ਕਾ ਜਸੁ ਗਾਵੀਜੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਤੁਝੁ ਸਭੁ ਕੋਊ ਮਾਨੈ॥

(ਅੰਗ 270)॥   ਮੁਖਿ ਤਾ ਕੋ ਜਸੁ ਰਸਨ ਬਖਾਨੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਤੇਰੋ ਰਹਤਾ ਧਰਮੁ॥ ਮਨ ਸਦਾ ਧਿਆਇ ਕੇਵਲ ਪਾਰਬ੍ਰਹਮੁ॥ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਜੀ ਜਪਤ ਦਰਗਹ ਮਾਨੁ ਪਾਵਹਿ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਪਤਿ ਸੇਤੀ ਘਰਿ ਜਾਵਹਿ॥ 2॥

Jih persaad(i) paat ptumbur hundaaveh(i).  Tiseh(i) tiyaag(i) kut uver loobhaaveh(i). Jih persaad(i) sookh sayj soyeejaiy.  Mun aatth pehr Ta ka jus(u) gaavijaiy.  Jih persaad(i) toojh(u) subh(u) koyoo maanaiy. 

(PAGE 270):        Mookh(i) Ta ko jus(u) rusn bukhaanaiy. Jih persaad(i) tayro rehta dherm(u). Mun suda dhiyaayay kayvul Parbraham(u). Prubh Ji juput Durgeh maan(u) paaveh(i). Nanak, put sayti ghur(i) jaaveh(i).   2.

Word Meaning:  Paat Ptumbur = silk clothes.     Tiyaag(i) = give up; ignoring.    Kut = where; what.   Uver = other.     Loobhaaveh(i) = getting captivated; fascinated.    Aatth pehr = twenty-four hours.   Maanaiy = give respect.   Dherm(u) = faith; principles.    Kayvul = only.   Ghur(i) = permanent residence (union with Waheguru).   2.

MEANING: Ignoring Waheguru, by whose grace you wear silk clothes, what else are you getting fascinated with, oh man? Sing praise all the time of Waheguru by whose grace you enjoy sleep on a comfortable bed, oh my mind.  Sing praise of Waheguru with your tongue, by whose grace you are given respect by others.  Recite only the Naam of Waheguru all the time by whose grace you are able to maintain your moral principles, oh man.  Worshipping Waheguru, the devotee is honoured in His Court and gets united with Him with dignity, oh Nanak.  2.

ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਆਰੋਗ ਕੰਚਨ ਦੇਹੀ॥ ਲਿਵ ਲਾਵਹੁ ਤਿਸੁ ਰਾਮ ਸਨੇਹੀ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਤੇਰਾ ਓਲਾ ਰਹਤ॥ ਮਨ ਸੁਖੁ ਪਾਵਹਿ ਹਰਿ ਹਰਿ ਜਸੁ ਕਹਤ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਤੇਰੇ ਸਗਲ ਛਿਦ੍ਰ ਢਾਕੈ॥ ਮਨ ਸਰਨੀ ਪਰੁ ਠਾਕੁਰ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਤਾ ਕੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਤੁਝੁ ਕੋ ਨ ਪਹੂਚੈ॥ ਮਨ ਸਾਸਿ ਸਾਸਿ ਸਿਮਰਹੁ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਊਚੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਪਾਈ ਦ੍ਰੁਲਭ ਦੇਹ॥ ਨਾਨਕ ਤਾ ਕੀ ਭਗਤਿ ਕਰੇਹਿ॥ 3॥

Jih persad(i) aaroag, kunchun dayhi.  Liv laavhou Tis(u) Ram snayi.  Jih persaad(i) tayra ohla reht.  Mun sookh(u) paaveh(i) Hur(i) Hur(i) jus(u) keht. JIh persaad(i) tayray sugul chhidur dhaakaiy.  Mun sherni pur(u) Tthaakur Prubh ta kaiy.  Jih persaad(i) toojh(u) ko na puhoochaiy. Mun saas(i) saas(i) simerou Prubh oochaiy.  Jih persaad(i) payee doorlubh dayh.  Nanak ta kee bhugut(i) kureh(i).    3.

Word Meaning: Aaroag = disease-free; healthy.  Kunchun = (pure like) gold    Snayhi = loving.    Ohla = secrets.   Chhidur = weaknesses; short comings.   Dhaakaiy = covers.    Puhoochaiy = has access.   Doorlubh = not easily available.  3.

MEANING: Concentrate on the loving Waheguru by whose grace you enjoy gold-like pure (good) health, oh man.  Enjoy bliss by singing praise of Waheguru, by whose grace all your secrets (weaknesses) are protected, oh my mind.  Surrender to the Lord by whose grace your shortcomings are covered, oh my mind.  Recite the Naam of the highest Waheguru with every breath, by whose grace no evil has access to you, oh my mind.  Worship the Lord by whose grace you have acquired the precious human body, which is not easily available, oh Nanak.    3. 

ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਆਭੂਖਨ ਪਹਿਰੀਜੈ॥ ਮਨ ਤਿਸੁ ਸਿਮਰਤ ਕਿਉ ਆਲਸੁ ਕੀਜੈ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਅਸਵ ਹਸਤਿ ਅਸਵਾਰੀ॥ ਮਨ ਤਿਸੁ ਪ੍ਰਭ ਕਉ ਕਬਹੂ ਨ ਬਿਸਾਰੀ॥ ਜਿਹ ਪ੍ਰਸਾਦਿ ਬਾਗ ਮਿਲਖ ਧਨਾ॥ ਰਾਖੁ ਪਰੋਇ ਪ੍ਰਭੁ ਅਪੁਨੇ ਮਨਾ॥ ਜਿਨਿ ਤੇਰੀ ਮਨ ਬਨਤ ਬਨਾਈ॥ ਊਠਤ ਬੈਠਤ ਸਦ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਧਿਆਈ॥ ਤਿਸਹਿ ਧਿਆਇ ਜੋ ਏਕ ਅਲਖੈ॥ ਈਹਾ ਊਹਾ ਨਾਨਕ ਤੇਰੀ ਰਖੈ॥ 4॥

Jih persaad(i) aabhookhun pehreejaiy. Mun Tis(u) simrut kiyoun aalus(u) keejaiy. Jih persaad(i) baag, milkh dhuna. Raakh(u) proyay Prubh(u) apunay munaa. Jin(i) tayri mun bunut bunaayee. Ootthut baiytthut sud Tiseh(i) dhiyaayee.  Tiseh(i) dhiyaayay jo Aik alukhaiy.  Eehaan, oohaan, Nanak tayri rukhaiy.  4.

Word Meaning: Aabhookhun = jewellery.    Aalus(u) = laziness; indolence.  Ashuv = horses.    Hust(i) = elephants.    Bisaari = forget.   Milkh = estate.   Proyay = stitched.    Bunut = creation.     Ootthut = standing.   Alukhaiy = incomprehensible.   Eehaan Oohaan = here and there (this world and the next).    4.
